Point the statusline (=claude-code-ide-org--statusline-task-string=)
at the *queue* files' latest event across all sessions, rather than
=org-clocking-p=/=org-clock-marker=, once those stop being live in
real time. Arguably more honest than today's version regardless of
this refactor's fate — reflects "what Claude is doing" rather than
"what's been recorded," and can't desync the way marker-based state
can, since it's just reading append-only files.

Explicit instruction, added 2026-08-06: don't stop at a bare
event-name swap. Now that a pending-queue-inspection tool
([[id:63a642c7-b04d-42dc-b6ec-3fb13df3ae04][63a642c7]]) exists in this
same plan — and, since
[[id:c9292ce8-8fb5-41c0-8de1-bbf74ffa7fce][c9292ce8]] was cancelled,
is the *only* place queue state gets surfaced outside the review
command — consider whether the statusline should surface that
same information glanceably from the terminal — e.g. a pending-review
count ("3 updates awaiting review"), or a visible distinction between
"applied" and "queued, not yet reviewed" for whatever heading it
names — rather than just relocating today's single clocked-in/out
label to a different data source with the same one-line shape.

*Third requirement, folded in from
[[id:4cda6bf7-441b-47b2-b3d5-b8c7e7a207a5][4cda6bf7]] on 2026-08-14,
which was cancelled in favour of this heading:* the label must
distinguish the *states*, not just the clock. Today's binary collapses
four distinct situations into two words — =PLANNING= and =DOING= both
read "clocked in" though one is still designing and the other
implementing; =WAIT= and =DONE= both read "clocked out" though one is
blocked and the other finished.

That heading proposed getting the keyword from =org-get-todo-state= at
the clock marker. *Do not do that here* — it is the reason it was
cancelled rather than merged as-is. The on-disk keyword lags the queue
by an entire review pass, so it would report a heading as =TODO= while
this session has already queued =DOING= on it. The keyword this
statusline should show is *the one the queue last recorded*, which is
the same source everything else in this heading reads from, and which is
also the only version that answers "what is Claude attending to" rather
than "what did the last review pass write down."

Practical consequence for whoever builds this: the five existing
=--statusline-task-string= regression tests assert on the literal
"clocked in"/"clocked out" strings and will need rewriting. Doing both
changes here means rewriting them once rather than twice.

*Promoted out of [[id:b5f7c5c7-7ad6-4c68-9cce-3479db1f1644][b5f7c5c7]] on
2026-08-14.* A capability the epic *enables* rather than one it
requires — the same test that promoted
[[id:5ff5a4b8-2fee-472a-b5e1-c9de8cf96704][5ff5a4b8]] and
[[id:d1cf852a-696a-4195-a4a7-039439c1f4b7][d1cf852a]] out on 2026-08-13.
Decoupling live visibility is worth doing whether or not the epic ever
closes, and the epic closes whether or not this is done.

[[file:~/.claude/plans/elegant-questing-lamport.md][Plan]] — the epic's plan
document, which carries this child's design rather than a file of its own.

:END:
*Verified, not just implemented* — shipped 2026-08-18 as step 1 of
[[id:406e9200-c3e7-49ac-a8d0-d6ff8e061ce4][406e9200]].

=--statusline-task-string= now asks the queue first, via two new helpers:
=--queue-latest-session-id= (newest queue by mtime, so one file is read
rather than every session's) and =--statusline-queue-state= (the heading
named by the newest id-bearing event, plus whether the newest guidepost is
a =resume=). It reads with INCLUDE-CONSUMED on purpose — an *applied*
event is the strongest evidence of what was being worked on, and filtering
to pending would blank the statusline right after every review pass.

*The live check is the one worth keeping.* After reload, with
=org-clocking-p= returning *nil* — no live clock anywhere — the statusline
still read =Decouple live "what is Claude… [290b6fc5] (clocked in, 0:07
total)=. That is precisely the regression this heading exists to prevent,
demonstrated *before*
[[id:226ed53b-c302-4529-870f-a42962b50bde][226ed53b]] silences the
trigger.

*A defect in the first draft, caught by the existing suite.* It returned
the empty string when the queue named a heading that would not resolve.
Wrong: an archived or deleted id is exactly that case, and the queue
having an opinion is not the same as that opinion being usable. Five
statusline tests failed — because they run against the real
=~/.claude/org-updates= and so the queue *did* have something to say. The
fix falls through to the clock when the queue id does not resolve.

*Test honesty:* three tests added, two of which fail when the change is
stashed. The third asserts the fallback and cannot discriminate, since the
old code read the clock unconditionally; it is a characterisation test and
is labelled as one.

*Not done here, and deliberately:* the second half of this heading's
original brief — surfacing pending-review state glanceably in the
statusline — is untouched. This landed only what step 2 requires.


** DONE The session-start report's "DOING, not clocked" line has stopped discriminating :code:
CLOSED: [2026-08-21 Fri 08:31]
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       e0904e93-b99d-44ad-b641-e3e13aced8c4
:CREATED:  [2026-08-18 Tue 11:41]
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
- State "TODO"       from              [2026-08-18 Tue 11:41] \\
  found while silencing the auto-clock trigger
CLOCK: [2026-08-18 Tue 12:10]--[2026-08-18 Tue 12:12] =>  0:02
- [2026-08-18 Tue 12:10]--[2026-08-18 Tue 12:12] assigned at review
- State "NEXT"       from "TODO"       [2026-08-19 Wed 18:56] \\
  Auto-promoted: sole remaining TODO in its sibling group.
CLOCK: [2026-08-21 Fri 08:31]--[2026-08-21 Fri 08:32] =>  0:01
- State "DONE"       from "DOING"      [2026-08-21 Fri 08:31] \\
  shipped, mutation-tested, verified live
- State "DOING"      from "NEXT"       [2026-08-21 Fri 08:31] \\
  re-key session report on queue divergence
- [2026-08-21 Fri 08:31]--[2026-08-21 Fri 08:32] re-key session report on queue divergence
:END:
:PLAN:

=claude-code-ide-org--attention-headings-context= reports a leaf as
"DOING, not clocked" when it is not the heading =org-clock-marker= points
at. The comment beside its sibling exclusion states the standard the line
has to meet: "a report that always says the same thing stops being read,
which defeats the only purpose this one has."

It no longer meets it. The exclusion is keyed on a *live* clock, and
after [[id:226ed53b-c302-4529-870f-a42962b50bde][226ed53b]]'s step 2(c)
nothing holds one open outside a review pass, so every DOING leaf matches
every time. This session's own =SessionStart= showed two such lines and
no "Currently clocked in" line at all.

*Not a regression 2(c) introduced, which is the point worth recording.*
The 2026-08-11 cutover already stopped the MCP tools from clocking; the
trigger was the last producer, and it only ever fired on a hand-edit. So
the line has been drifting toward constant for a week and 2(c) merely
finishes the drift. Nothing to roll back — the input the exclusion reads
is simply gone.

*Not yet decided:* what the line should key on instead. The obvious
candidate is the queue rather than the marker, the same move
[[id:290b6fc5-3ebb-4827-9a97-5a3542e354b6][290b6fc5]] made for the
statusline — "has this heading seen a guidepost recently" is answerable
from the queue files and is closer to what the report is actually trying
to say. The alternative is that the line has no job left and should go.
Deciding needs a look at what a session actually wants told to it, not
just a mechanical port of the predicate.

*Decided and shipped 2026-08-21: keep the line, re-key it on file-versus-queue
divergence, and retire the word "clocked".* The body left two options open --
port the predicate to the queue, or conclude the line has no job. Neither was
taken as written. The queue port was right about the *source* and wrong about
the *question*: "has this heading seen a guidepost recently" is still a
statement about activity, and the report's actual job is to say where the file
and the record disagree.

=claude-code-ide-org--attention-headings-context= now emits three kinds, backed
by a new =claude-code-ide-org--queue-heading-divergence= that maps each heading
:ID: to its most recent *pending* =todo= event and the timestamp of its most
recent pending event of any kind:

- =DOING in file, queued -> X, not yet applied= -- the file is behind.
- =queued DOING, not yet applied= -- the queue is ahead, and the file may carry
  no keyword at all.
- =DOING in file, nothing queued since <ts>= -- genuinely walked away from,
  using the same before-today test
  =claude-code-ide-org-write-session-start-report= already uses, so both reports
  answer "first thing each day" the same way.

*It discriminates by construction*, which is the standard this heading was
opened to restore: it goes silent exactly when the file and the queue agree.
Pending events only -- an applied event is one the file already matches, so
counting it would report a divergence at the instant it was resolved.

:END:
*Verified live against the real files and the real queue, after =load-file=* --
the running Emacs held the old definition and would otherwise have answered for
it. Two lines, both true and neither constant: 420816ec as =queued DOING, not
yet applied ... file says TODO=, and befaed0a as =DOING in file, queued -> DONE,
not yet applied=. The two DOING containers were correctly silent. Under the old
predicate the first was unreportable and the second read only "not clocked".

*Both directions were live specimens on the day, which is why both are
implemented.* The old line named befaed0a, which was finished; it said nothing
about c6fc6f46, which was keywordless in the file while a session actively
worked it.

*Verified by mutation, not by a green suite.* Three mutations each fail the new
test: deleting the file-behind branch, deleting the queue-ahead branch, and
making the abandoned branch unconditional -- the last being the old always-on
behaviour restored, so the test fails if the report ever goes constant again.
330/330 with the file restored.

*One boundary left deliberately unreported.* A keywordless heading whose queue
holds a pending =DONE= is a real divergence and is *not* reported: by then the
heading is finished as far as the record is concerned, and "the queue has
unapplied items" is =org_pending_updates='s question, not this report's.
c6fc6f46 was in exactly that state by the end of the session and stayed silent,
correctly.

*One test changed, and the reason matters more than the wording.*
=...-includes-abandoned-doing-leaf= asserted the old string; it now asserts
=nothing ever queued for it= and is wrapped in
=claude-code-ide-org-test--with-queue=. Without that wrapper it read the user's
real =~/.claude/org-updates=, which the report's new input made load-bearing --
a suite that consults live user state can flake on it.


** DONE An unassigned span's group heading renders an error string where a title belongs :code:
CLOSED: [2026-08-18 Tue 19:47]
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       98700ea3-6770-429b-84dd-1fddde60afed
:CREATED:  [2026-08-18 Tue 16:00]
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
- State "TODO"       from              [2026-08-18 Tue 16:00] \\
  cosmetic review-buffer rendering defect
- State "DONE"       from "TODO"       [2026-08-18 Tue 19:47] \\
  fixed, tested, confirmed live
:END:
:PLAN:

Observed live 2026-08-18 in =org_pending_updates=, grouping two
unassigned spans:

: Error: no org heading found with :ID: "nil"  {nil}
:   ! span  - [2026-08-18 Tue 12:10]--[2026-08-18 Tue 12:12] ... UNASSIGNED

An unassigned span carries =:id nil= until a human presses =a=, which is
the normal, expected state for it — not an error. But the grouping code
resolves the id to build the group's title, and =--at-id= *returns* its
failure message as a string rather than signalling, so the message lands
where a title belongs.

*This is [[id:c2132d3f-dd78-4498-ba87-0ca814dc2f77][c2132d3f]] again by a
different route.* That heading fixed exactly this shape — an =--at-id=
error string rendered as a group heading — for an :ID: that does not
resolve, and its own note explains the mechanism: "a =cond= clause of the
form =((--at-id ...))= yields its own test, and =--at-id= returns its
error rather than signalling". The nil-id path was not covered, and it is
the one case where the missing id is *routine* rather than a defect, so
it is the case that shows up most.

Cosmetic — the spans are assignable with =a= and nothing is lost. Worth
fixing because an "Error:" in the review buffer trains the reader to
ignore errors there, which is the opposite of what that column is for.

*Filed here for findability, not because it belongs to this epic's goal.*
It was found while verifying
[[id:31f766ab-90ed-4950-bc5b-787528c8fe84][31f766ab]] and is independent
of the guidepost pipeline; refile freely.

:END:
*Fixed 2026-08-18* (=4ff1b53=). The grouping code now uses
=claude-code-ide-org--review-heading-title=, which returns *nil* when an
id does not resolve — which is what lets a =cond= tell three cases
apart: unassigned, resolvable, unresolvable. The review buffer already
had that =cond= from
[[id:c2132d3f-dd78-4498-ba87-0ca814dc2f77][c2132d3f]]; only
=org_pending_updates= was left on =--at-id=.

*The test did not discriminate on the first attempt, and the reason is
worth keeping.* It asserted =(string-match-p "unassigned -- press"
report)=, which passed with the fix mutated away — because
=string-match-p= honours =case-fold-search=, defaulting to =t=, so it
was matching the *item* line's =UNASSIGNED -- press `a'...= rather than
the group heading at all. Now binds =case-fold-search= nil and anchors
on the opening paren, which only the group line carries. Caught by
mutating the branch away, not by reading the test.

That is the second fake assertion this practice caught today — the other
was in [[id:507754ba-ae82-4f04-b209-8d8c58ac268e][507754ba]], where a
window-extension test asserted only that a line looked plausible.


** TODO Concurrent sessions' turns must union, not sum, wherever time is totalled :code:
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       7d739afd-f879-445c-b0b6-65e295585a94
:CREATED:  [2026-08-18 Tue 16:36]
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
- State "TODO"       from              [2026-08-19 Wed 08:05] \\
  re-queued, lost to my git checkout
:END:

Stated by the user 2026-08-18: two sessions running at once produce
turns that overlap in wall-clock time, and those must *union*, not sum.
=[10:00]--[10:20]= plus =[10:10]--[10:30]= is *30 minutes*, not 40 --
one stretch of elapsed time during which the agent was busy, not two
separate stretches laid end to end.

*The primitive already exists and is unwired.*
=claude-code-ide-org--merge-time-intervals= sorts
=(START . END)= conses and merges anything adjacent or overlapping into
a min-to-max span -- exactly this operation. It has carried an
"UNWIRED as of 2026-08-10" note ever since. So this is a wiring
decision, not a design one, and nothing needs coining.

*Where it already bites, found while scoping
[[id:507754ba-ae82-4f04-b209-8d8c58ac268e][507754ba]]:* pooling
guideposts from several sessions and running
=claude-code-ide-org--span-work-runs= over the merged stream does not
merely double-count -- it *mangles* the pairing. Sorted, two overlapping
turns read =resume, resume, pause, pause=, and the run finder pairs only
the inner =resume= -> =pause=, yielding 10 minutes where the
truth is 30. Too high and too low are both reachable from the same defect,
depending on the interleaving.

The fix wherever runs are computed over multiple sessions is the same
shape: compute runs *per session*, then union the results. Never pool
the events first.

*Not the same as the overlap note in*
[[id:226ed53b-c302-4529-870f-a42962b50bde][226ed53b]], which proposes
apply refusing to write a span overlapping an existing CLOCK line on the
same heading. That is one heading's record disagreeing with itself; this
is two sessions' real, simultaneous work being counted twice.

*Open:* whether the union applies at the CLOCK-line level (never write
overlapping lines) or only at report time (clocktables and totals
de-duplicate). Those differ when two sessions genuinely work on two
*different* headings at once -- each heading's own interval is honestly
its own, and only the sum across headings overstates elapsed time. That
distinction has not been decided and should not be decided by whoever
happens to touch this first.

*Vindicated 2026-08-21, having nearly been retired.*
[[id:9202b39d-49e4-441c-89f2-382feb4ef1ea][9202b39d]] proposed a depth counter
over the merged stream and claimed it "gives 7d739afd's union for free, so one
mechanism answers both" -- which, taken at face value, retires this heading's
central instruction. It was taken at face value, in conversation, before anyone
ran it.

Running it settles the matter the other way. Over the 844-guidepost corpus the
depth counter returns 13.61 h against this heading's per-session-then-union at
22.79 h, because it latches: one missing =pause= leaves depth permanently above
zero and no run is ever emitted again. Full numbers are in 9202b39d's body.
*"Compute runs per session, then union the results. Never pool the events first"
stands as written* -- pooling is exactly what fails, and it fails for the reason
stated here in the first place.

*The magnitude of this heading's own concern, measured for the first time.*
Across the same 10-day corpus, per-session runs sum to 22.79 h and union to
22.68 h. The double-count this heading exists to prevent is *0.11 h -- 0.5%*.
Real, reproducible, and small.

That number is an argument about *where*, not *whether*. At 0.5% there is no
case for paying for the union at write time, where the cost of being wrong is a
CLOCK line that was refused and cannot be recovered. The report-time half is
recomputable, reversible, and already written:
=claude-code-ide-org--merge-time-intervals= has carried an "UNWIRED as of
2026-08-10" note for eleven days and does precisely this operation.

*The open question above is deliberately still open.* Its own words -- the
CLOCK-line-versus-report-time call "should not be decided by whoever happens to
touch this first" -- and the ratification that would have closed it was
conditional on a verification that failed. What the measurement adds is a
recommendation with a number behind it, not a decision: wire
=--merge-time-intervals= wherever a total is taken *across* headings, leave
per-heading CLOCK lines written as computed, and let the 0.5% decide whether
even that is worth doing now.


** TODO A background job's first turn is bracketed across two session_ids :code:
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       9202b39d-49e4-441c-89f2-382feb4ef1ea
:CREATED:  [2026-08-18 Tue 16:41]
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
- State "TODO"       from              [2026-08-19 Wed 08:06] \\
  re-queued, lost to my git checkout
CLOCK: [2026-08-21 Fri 08:20]--[2026-08-21 Fri 08:32] =>  0:12
- [2026-08-21 Fri 08:20]--[2026-08-21 Fri 08:32] depth-counter verification, reassigned by hand from 420816ec
:END:

Measured 2026-08-18 while recomputing the accumulated record for
[[id:507754ba-ae82-4f04-b209-8d8c58ac268e][507754ba]]. Today's
guideposts, grouped by =session_id=:

| session_id | resume | pause | first    | last     |
|------------+--------+-------+----------+----------|
| =0c504277= |     21 |    21 | 07:14:33 | 10:45:46 |
| =330316ca= |      1 |     0 | 10:47:22 | 10:47:22 |
| =6f28d85e= |      9 |     8 | 11:24:35 | 16:35:15 |

=330316ca= is the interactive session; its single =resume= at 10:47:22
is the =UserPromptSubmit= that *launched a background job*. =6f28d85e=
is that job, and its stream opens with the matching =pause= at 11:24:35
-- 37 minutes later, the end of the job's first turn.

So a background job's first turn has its =resume= in the launching
session's queue file and its =pause= in the job's. The two halves of one
real turn are in different files.

*What this breaks.* Any computation that pairs =resume= -> =pause=
*within* a session loses that turn entirely. In the recompute it cost
37 minutes on one CLOCK line, silently, and the line corrected to
=0:00= -- a plausible-looking answer with nothing to flag it. The fix
adopted there is a depth counter over the merged stream rather than
per-session pairing: =resume= increments, =pause= decrements, busy while
depth > 0. That also happens to give
[[id:7d739afd-f879-445c-b0b6-65e295585a94][7d739afd]]'s union for free,
so one mechanism answers both.

*This is a third explanation for
[[id:09c134c4-31eb-4f6c-aa10-f836d1d28f16][09c134c4]]'s unmatched
resumes*, and it is not the one that heading proposes. Its hypothesis is
"an interrupt or a queued follow-up" -- two =UserPromptSubmit= with no
intervening =Stop=. Here the =Stop= exists; it is simply filed elsewhere.
Worth checking how many of the 8 unmatched resumes are job launches
before instrumenting anything, since that costs nothing and this one is
already confirmed.

*Possible consequence, not verified:* CLAUDE.md says the =ExitPlanMode=
cross-session guard "comes free from the file being per-session --
another session's =PLANNING= is simply not in it to be found". If a
background job is launched by a turn that queued =PLANNING=, that event
is in the launching session's file and the job's hook reads its own, so
the promotion would not fire. Narrow -- it needs =PLANNING= set by the
very turn that launches a job -- and it has not been reproduced. Filed
as a question, not a defect.

*Verified 2026-08-21 against the raw queue corpus* -- 844 guideposts, 13
sessions, 2026-08-11 to 2026-08-21. The two halves of this heading came apart:
the measurement holds exactly, the proposed mechanism does not.

*The measurement is confirmed, independently and to the second.* Reproduced
straight from the =.jsonl= files: =330316ca='s lone =resume= at 10:47:22 with no
=pause=, =6f28d85e='s stream opening with a =pause= at 11:24:35, gap 37 minutes.
The table above is right and nothing in it needs correcting.

*The depth counter is falsified, and not marginally.* Implemented exactly as
this body specifies -- merged stream, =resume= increments, =pause= decrements,
busy while depth > 0 -- it produces *13.61 h* against per-session adjacency's
*22.79 h*. It loses 9.18 h.

*The failure is a latch, which is why it is disqualifying rather than tunable.*
Depth has no way back down once an expected =pause= never arrives. At
=2026-08-17 13:09:03= depth reached 2 and never returned to 0 again for the
remaining 3.8 days of the corpus, climbing to 9 and ending there. After that
instant *no run is ever emitted*, because a run only closes when depth hits
zero. The corpus supplies 11 unmatched =resume= -> =resume= adjacencies and 2
sessions whose stream ends on an unclosed =resume=; any one of them is enough,
permanently.

*Compare the failure modes, which is the whole argument.* Per-session adjacency
degrades *locally* -- a lost =pause= costs exactly one run, and the next
=resume= starts clean. The depth counter degrades *unboundedly and silently* --
one lost =pause= corrupts every subsequent day. A pipeline whose stated goal is
durations that mean what they say cannot adopt the second.

*Tested generously and it still fails.* Resetting the counter daily -- the
framing under which the one-off =507754ba= recompute would have looked correct,
since a single day's stream is usually balanced -- gives 19.95 h against 22.79
h, and errs in *both directions*: =-2.63 h= on 08-17, =+1.85 h= on 08-19. The
08-19 overcount is the one that settles it. Depth held above zero across an idle
gap and re-absorbed the human's thinking time -- which is precisely the ~45%
overcount [[id:406e9200-c3e7-49ac-a8d0-d6ff8e061ce4][406e9200]] exists to
eliminate. The mechanism reintroduces its own parent's defect.

*The obvious repair also latches.* Keying the open set on =session_id= instead
of counting makes a duplicate =resume= idempotent, so unmatched resumes stop
accumulating -- but the 2 sessions that never close still pin the set open
forever. 18.46 h; better, still 4.33 h short, still latching.

*So "one mechanism answers both" is withdrawn.* This heading and
[[id:7d739afd-f879-445c-b0b6-65e295585a94][7d739afd]] need two mechanisms, and
each is cheap on its own. That heading's "compute runs per session, then union
the results, never pool the events first" is *vindicated* by the same run, not
superseded.

*What survives here is a targeted linking rule, not a counter.* A session whose
stream *opens* with a =pause= is a background job whose first turn began
elsewhere; pair that =pause= with the nearest preceding unclosed =resume= in
another session. Measured over the corpus: 3 sessions open with a =pause=, the
rule fires once, recovers *0.62 h* -- exactly the 37 minutes above -- and
correctly leaves the other two alone, since neither had any open =resume= to
pair with.

*One caveat, recorded because the corpus could not test it.* The implementation
picks the *latest* open resume when several are candidates, and every case the
corpus presented had exactly one. Multi-candidate ambiguity is untested; treat
the rule as measured, not as general, and re-check it the first time two
sessions have unclosed resumes when a background job's pause lands.

:END:
:PLAN:

=claude-code-ide-org-span-idle-floor= is a named =defcustom=. The floor
on what gets *written* is not: it is two conditions inside
=claude-code-ide-org--apply-idle-floor= — drop when the rendered
endpoints share a minute, or when the duration rounds to =0:00= — giving
an effective minimum near 30s. Shortest run actually written: 39s.

Give it a name and a default preserving today's behaviour, so both
floors have the same shape and either can be tuned without editing code.

*The value is deliberately not set here.* Measured 2026-08-19 over the
272 runs the current code writes (28.36 h), for whoever decides later:

| cutoff | lines dropped | time dropped |
|--------+---------------+--------------|
| 60s    | 25 (9.2%)     | 0.34 h (1.2%) |
| 120s   | 89 (32.7%)    | 1.84 h (6.5%) |
| 180s   | 127 (46.7%)   | 3.36 h (11.9%) |

24% of written lines currently read =>  0:01=. A 120s floor buys
symmetry with the idle floor and a one-number rule of thumb, at 6.5% of
recorded time. Decide against a month of data, not these eight days —
[[id:96a51c2f-ce11-4b40-bdff-11223d3d4eb7][96a51c2f]].

*Two mechanisms considered and rejected*, both raised 2026-08-19: counting
=[10:00]--[10:01]= as two minutes puts a =>= total on the line that
=org-clock-sum= contradicts from the same timestamps; bumping an endpoint
outward fabricates time — about an hour across the 65 lines that would move —
which is [[id:b74e0f19-5a26-4c83-9d70-8e1c5a2f6b04][b74e0f19]]'s rounding
incident pointed the other way.

:END:
*Shipped 2026-08-20 as =claude-code-ide-org-span-minimum-interval=, defaulting
to 0.* Zero is the point: it reproduces today's behaviour exactly, so naming the
floor moved nothing. What had been holding the line was two rendering conditions
inside =claude-code-ide-org--apply-idle-floor= — endpoints inside one minute, or
a duration rounding to =0:00= — which are consequences of the clock format, not
a policy anyone chose. Both remain; the new variable is a third condition that
does nothing until set.

*Verified by mutation, not by a green suite.* Three separate mutations each fail
a distinct assertion: deleting the drop condition, defaulting it to 120, and
relaxing =<= to =<==. Strictly-less matches
=claude-code-ide-org-span-idle-floor=, so the two read the same way at their
boundary.

*Measured for September rather than decided now.* Post-cutover corpus, 283 lines
/ 36.88 h: 120s drops 23.7% of lines for 3.03% of recorded time; 180s, 40.6% for
7.37%; 300s, 63.6% for 17.71%; 60s drops nothing, confirming the rendering
conditions already cover that range. The sweep lives in the docstring and the
value belongs to [[id:96a51c2f-ce11-4b40-bdff-11223d3d4eb7][96a51c2f]].

*The measurement that did not replicate is the more useful half.* 96a51c2f
recorded 6.5% of recorded time for the same 120s value on 2026-08-18; the same
measurement on 2026-08-20 gives 3.03%. No code changed — the corpus grew and the
mix shifted. That heading's instruction to re-derive rather than confirm now has
a two-day counterexample behind it, and its body was corrected to say so.


** DONE The review buffer predicts a duration org does not write :code:
CLOSED: [2026-08-21 Fri 07:52]
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       c6fc6f46-4954-4fdf-9d7e-7c8bf3a1a952
:CREATED:  [2026-08-20 Thu 16:43]
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
CLOCK: [2026-08-20 Thu 16:28]--[2026-08-20 Thu 16:29] =>  0:01
- [2026-08-20 Thu 16:28]--[2026-08-20 Thu 16:29] assigned at review
CLOCK: [2026-08-20 Thu 16:41]--[2026-08-20 Thu 16:46] =>  0:05
- [2026-08-20 Thu 16:41]--[2026-08-20 Thu 16:46] assigned at review
- State "TODO"       from              [2026-08-20 Thu 16:45] \\
  captured keywordless; state never set
CLOCK: [2026-08-21 Fri 07:48]--[2026-08-21 Fri 07:52] =>  0:04
- State "DOING"      from "TODO"       [2026-08-21 Fri 07:48] \\
  one root cause, three sites; 56 written lines already self-inconsistent
- [2026-08-21 Fri 07:48]--[2026-08-21 Fri 07:52]
- State "DONE"       from "DOING"      [2026-08-21 Fri 07:52] \\
  root cause fixed at all three sites; the 56 stale lines left for a separate approval
:END:
:PLAN:

=claude-code-ide-org--review-written-summary= sums raw seconds across a span's
runs. org computes a CLOCK line's duration from the two *minute-truncated*
timestamps it records. The two disagree by a minute whenever the seconds fall
either side of a boundary.

Caught on a real line, 2026-08-20. The review buffer previewed =writes 0:02= for
the 10:19--10:22 span; apply wrote =CLOCK:
[2026-08-20 Thu 10:19]--[2026-08-20 Thu 10:22] => 0:03=. Guideposts were
10:19:49 and 10:22:18 -- 149s, which rounds to 2, while org subtracts 10:19 from
10:22 and gets 3.

*Measured across the whole queue corpus:* of 361 runs where either method writes
a minute, *25.5% disagree* -- 43 runs (11.9%) where org writes one minute less
than previewed, 49 (13.6%) where it writes one more. Near-symmetric, so
aggregate totals barely move; the error is per-line and does not cancel for a
human reading one line.

*This is the function failing its own stated contract*, which is what makes it
worth fixing rather than tolerating. Its docstring says it returns "what apply
will *really* write", and the reasoning recorded there is that confirming a
number which exists nowhere is worse than the overcount it replaced. A
prediction that is wrong a quarter of the time reintroduces exactly that
failure, one minute at a time.

*Approach:* compute the preview the way org does -- truncate each run's
endpoints to the minute, then subtract -- rather than summing float seconds.
That also makes the preview agree with =--apply-idle-floor='s own
rendered-endpoint test, which already truncates, so the two would stop reasoning
about time in different units.

*Verify:* a test pinning a run of 10:19:49--10:22:18 to a preview of =0:03=, and
failing on the current =0:02=.

/Body restored 2026-08-20 by =org_amend=; the original capture silently
discarded it -- see/ [[id:1cd2df62-35e9-47dc-bcce-dda7bae3c522][1cd2df62]].

:END:
*Fixed 2026-08-21, and the reported symptom was the smaller half.* Chasing the
root cause found that *56 of the 353 CLOCK lines standing in TODO.org and
DONE.org disagree with their own printed timestamps* -- 35 printing a minute
less than their stamps imply, 21 a minute more. Same cause: raw seconds summed
and rounded, where org derives a line's total by subtracting two
minute-precision stamps.

*Nothing could have surfaced those 56.* A clocktable recomputes from the
timestamps and never reads the =>= field, so =org_clock_report= has been quietly
reporting different totals than the drawer a human was reading, with no error
anywhere. The lines are *not* repaired here -- that is a data change and belongs
to its own approved step.

New =claude-code-ide-org--clock-minutes= truncates both endpoints before
subtracting; =--format-clock-line= derives its total from it, so a line agrees
with its own stamps by construction. The preview computes *per run* rather than
pooling, because org writes each line separately and summing raw seconds first
also invents a minute out of combined remainders.

*Two of the three sites were live, one was not, and saying which matters.* The
preview and =--format-clock-line= via the recompute caller are where the 56 came
from. =claude-code-ide-org-close-open-interval= and the consolidate caller are
fed endpoints already parsed from org timestamps, so raw rounding was exact
there today -- routed through the helper anyway so the invariant survives either
input gaining seconds. *Latent, not fixed.*

*One existing test had to be rewritten rather than re-expected, which is the
more interesting finding.* =test-recompute-window-reaches-past-the-minute=
pinned the =>= figure to prove the 60-second window extension mattered. Under
correct arithmetic it cannot: any guidepost inside that extension truncates to
the same minute as END *by construction*, so it can never move a total. Verified
by removing the extension -- byte-identical output, exactly the "verified
nothing" failure that test's own docstring was written to warn about. It now
asserts on *membership* instead: a lone =pause= at 09:10:30 drops the line when
the window includes it and leaves it whole when it does not, a difference no
rounding coincidence can imitate.

*Known limit, flagged not chased.* Even corrected, the preview cannot model the
already-recorded-interval subtraction apply performs at point, because an
unassigned span has no heading to read a drawer from. The truncation fix removes
the 25.5% class; that residual is inherent to previewing before assignment.

Mutation-tested three ways -- reverting to raw rounding, truncating only one
endpoint, and removing the window extension each fail a distinct test. 329
tests.


** DONE Fifty-six CLOCK lines disagree with their own timestamps :code:
CLOSED: [2026-08-21 Fri 08:01]
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       057cec53-d10e-45d0-b9ef-32713411c439
:CREATED:  [2026-08-21 Fri 07:52]
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
- State "TODO"       from              [2026-08-21 Fri 07:53] \\
  data repair, awaiting approval; writer already fixed
CLOCK: [2026-08-21 Fri 08:00]--[2026-08-21 Fri 08:01] =>  0:01
- State "DOING"      from "TODO"       [2026-08-21 Fri 08:00] \\
  approved; all 56 are post-cutover so the era question is moot
- [2026-08-21 Fri 08:00]--[2026-08-21 Fri 08:01]
- State "DONE"       from "DOING"      [2026-08-21 Fri 08:01] \\
  56 repaired, 0 remaining; all post-cutover
:END:
:PLAN:

Measured 2026-08-21: of 353 CLOCK lines across TODO.org and DONE.org, *56 print
a =>= total that does not match their own two timestamps* -- 35 a minute low, 21
a minute high. Example, TODO.org:184: =[...09:31]--[...09:36] => 0:04=, where
the stamps say five minutes.

The cause is fixed ([[id:c6fc6f46-4954-4fdf-9d7e-7c8bf3a1a952][c6fc6f46]]) and
no new line can be written this way. These are the ones already on disk.

*Why nobody noticed, and why it still matters.* A clocktable recomputes from the
timestamps and never reads the =>= field, so =org_clock_report= has been
silently disagreeing with the drawer a human reads. Neither number is corrupt;
they simply cannot both be right, and the *timestamps are the record* -- they
are what org itself computes from, and what every report already uses.

*Deliberately not repaired at the same time as the code.* Rewriting the
accumulated record in bulk is the shape behind
[[id:79c224ab-188a-4ed4-a606-c28ee2cfe72d][79c224ab]] and the =b74e0f19=
rounding incident, and the one comparable pass (the =507754ba= recompute) was
explicitly approved first. Fixing the writer is safe on its own; rewriting
history is a separate decision with a separate blast radius.

*Approach when approved.* A targeted totals-only rewrite: for each matching
CLOCK line, recompute =>= from the two stamps already printed on it and replace
*only that field*. Not a =--consolidate-logbook-text= pass, which rewrites whole
drawers and is exactly the incident shape. The endpoints are never touched, so
no interval changes, only the derived label agrees with the interval again.

*Verification is already written* -- the same script that found the 56 re-run
after, expecting 0. Sequencing: commit first, write on disk with the buffers
unmodified, =revert-buffer= without =preserve-modes=, lint, separate commit.

*Open, and the reason this is not simply a chore.* Whether pre-cutover lines
should be repaired at all. They were written by the old aggregator against a
different model of what an interval meant, and
=claude-code-ide-org-span-minimum-interval='s docstring already treats them as a
population deliberately left alone. Making their labels self-consistent is
defensible; so is leaving them visibly of their era.

*Repaired 2026-08-21 on the user's approval, commit =7db9a4b=.* All 56 lines now
agree with their own timestamps; the finder re-run reports 353 CLOCK lines and 0
disagreements.

*The open question above answered itself.* All 56 turned out to be
*post-cutover* -- 36 in DONE.org, 20 in TODO.org -- so nothing written by the
old aggregator was touched, and the judgement about whether to leave pre-cutover
lines visibly of their era never had to be made. Worth recording as the reason,
rather than leaving the impression it was decided.

:END:
*Verified three ways rather than one.* The script that found the 56, re-run: 0
remaining. The diff: exactly 112 lines, 56 pairs, *no pair differing anywhere
before* =>= -- so no endpoint moved and no interval changed, only the derived
label. And no non-CLOCK line touched at all.

*Net effect: +14 minutes across 65.65 h*, 35 lines gaining a minute and 21
losing one. Small in aggregate, which is exactly what a near-symmetric rounding
error predicts -- and exactly why aggregate totals were never going to surface
this. The individual lines were wrong in both directions.

*One thing the repair confirmed about the earlier count.* 365 lines in the two
files carry a =>= H:MM= string but only 353 are CLOCK entries; the other twelve
are prose discussing clock lines, including this heading's own body. A
whole-line anchored pattern excluded them. A looser grep would have rewritten
documentation.


** TODO A split span drops the unowned gaps between the clock pairs that split it :code:
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       c54c4215-90e3-4e0c-9de1-990c0406a2b2
:CREATED:  [2026-08-25 Tue 18:44]
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
- State "TODO"       from              [2026-08-25 Tue 18:45] \\
  captured keywordless, setting initial state
:END:

*Found 2026-08-25 by the user*, who looked at two zero-minute spans left over
from a review pass and asked whether they marked the beginning and end of
meta-work. They do, and the duration between them is thrown away.

*The mechanism, from =--aggregate-guideposts='s own docstring rather than
inferred.* EXCLUSIONS -- today the =clock_in=/=clock_out= brackets that already
have an owner ([[id:eaeeb4ee-ceee-4094-8598-bbcdc8214b66][eaeeb4ee]]) -- are
treated like a permission block: one "lying wholly between two timestamps
*splits* the span rather than being clustered through". Split a two-timestamp
span twice and both timestamps are left alone, and "a lone timestamp yields a
zero-width span, which is honest".

*Each step is right and the composition loses time.* Not double-counting an
owned interval is correct. Refusing to claim a duration for a lone point is
correct. But splitting emits only the *endpoints* and never the *complement* --
the unowned gaps that lie between the exclusions inside the original span. Those
gaps are real, unattributed work, and nothing else will ever offer them.

*Today's window is a ready-made fixture, with every number checked against the
queue file:*

: guideposts  17:58:09 resume ... 18:13:44 pause      935 s apart
: threshold   claude-code-ide-org-guidepost-gap-threshold = 1200 s
: so          935 < 1200 -- these would have been ONE span
: exclusion   17:59:52--18:02:26  e1284bdb   154 s
: exclusion   18:05:44--18:12:36  f421c5c3   412 s
: emitted     [17:58]--[17:58] and [18:13]--[18:13], both zero-width
: dropped     935 - 566 = 369 s in three gaps

*The three dropped gaps, each identifiable from the transcript*, which is what
makes this a loss rather than a rounding artefact: 1:43 writing a measured note
onto [[id:1a5a5254-f62d-46e7-9a6c-3d84f2f52873][1a5a5254]]; 3:18 committing,
re-orienting onto the next heading and loading a skill; 1:08 composing the
session's closing report. All of it meta-work, and all of it exactly the residue
the day node exists to absorb.

*Note the threshold is not the cause*, which is worth stating because it is the
first thing anyone will suspect. At 935 s against a 1200 s threshold these
guideposts clustered fine; it is the exclusion split that separated them.
Raising the threshold would change nothing.

*Shape of the fix, not chosen:* emit the complement segments of (span minus
exclusions) instead of only the split endpoints. A span reduced to nothing by
its exclusions correctly yields no item at all; a span with interior gaps should
yield one item per gap. Whether a zero-width endpoint is still worth rendering
afterwards is a separate presentation question -- it may be that the endpoints
become redundant once the gaps are offered.

*Related but distinct.* [[id:2d100d0d-2d04-43b9-af1e-f4d6b52bbada][2d100d0d]] is
the opposite failure -- a span and an explicit interval both applying,
double-counting. This is what the fix for that traded away, so the two want
reading together.

*A second mechanism produces the same symptom, found 2026-08-26 and not
described above.* Three zero-width points survived a review pass, and only two
of them were splits.

: 18:36:39  split -- meta-work clock pair 18:37:22--18:39:19 lies between it and 18:42:11
: 20:54:33  split -- two owned pairs between it and 20:38:35 (7ae6562d, then aa1ba915)
: 19:36:14  NOT a split -- widowed by partial apply

*Widowing is the new one.* =19:36:14= sits 203 s from =19:32:51=, comfortably
inside the 1200 s threshold, with no exclusion between them -- they would have
clustered into a span of about 3:23. But =19:32:51= was *consumed by the apply
pass of 2026-08-26T08:08:06*, along with =19:17:53=, so the survivor has no
unconsumed neighbour left to cluster with and degenerates to a point.

*Read from the ledger, which could not have answered this a day earlier.* The
per-event apply time [[id:21c91613-76e0-4590-affe-e2af9f1321ca][21c91613]]
shipped is what makes "consumed by *which* pass" a lookup rather than a
reconstruction; six distinct passes are cleanly separable in the file. This is
that field's first real use, and it was used to diagnose a defect rather than to
undo anything.

*Why it matters more than the split case.* A split is created by the machinery
and is stable -- the same events always split the same way. Widowing is created
by *the human's own reviewing*, and it compounds: every partial apply can orphan
another survivor, so the zero-width points accumulate in proportion to how
carefully someone reviews. The more selectively the queue is applied, the more
residue it leaves.

*So the fix has two halves, not one.* Emitting the complement segments of (span
minus exclusions) addresses the splits. It does *not* address widowing, where
the missing neighbour is gone from the pending set entirely -- that needs the
aggregator to see consumed events when deciding whether a lone timestamp is
genuinely lone, which =--queue-events= already supports via =include-consumed=
and which =--review-suggest-heading= already uses for exactly that kind of
question.

*Fixture, recorded here so it survives dismissal.* Session =1ad547fe=,
guideposts at =2026-08-25T18:36:39=, =19:36:14= and =20:54:33=. Dismissing them
marks them consumed but *destroys nothing* -- =--queue-mark-dismissed= writes
beside the queue and never touches the =.jsonl=, so undoing it is deleting one
key from a small JSON file, and the events remain replayable.


** TODO Accepting a 0-run span records none of its minutes, and only =e= can claim them :code:
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       2deb090f-593d-40e3-9044-fda318d9b6de
:CREATED:  [2026-09-11 Fri 17:41]
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
CLOCK: [2026-09-11 Fri 17:41]--[2026-09-11 Fri 17:47] =>  0:06
- [2026-09-11 Fri 17:41]--[2026-09-11 Fri 17:47] check the 0-run span attention reasoning, file it
:END:

Assigning a 0-run span and applying it writes a =:LOGBOOK:= annotation and no
=CLOCK:= line at all — =--review-apply-clock='s =(unless observed ...)= branch —
so the envelope is recorded as zero. Measured 2026-09-11 on the ten pending
spans: seven have no run, including
[2026-09-11 Fri 13:24]--[2026-09-11 Fri 13:46], and every one of them goes from
0s to its full envelope the moment =:suggested= is cleared. The user's position,
stated the same day: a 0-run/0-turn span they choose to accept should carry all
those minutes, because accepting it *is* the assertion that the time was their
attention. Today the only way to say that is =e= — retyping both timestamps
unchanged purely to set =:suggested nil= — so the act costs two timestamps and
reads as an edit rather than a claim. Proposed: a way to accept the envelope as
attention in one act, distinct from correcting bounds; note that the bracket
style is a red herring here, since =:active= reaches only the annotation while
the =CLOCK:= line is always written inactive by =org-clock-in=/=org-clock-out=.
Reads with [[id:01849bef-fdc9-4c30-b467-00ca8606e8e0][01849bef]] (the same
keystroke family, and the same question of what the assertion means),
[[id:996fd2bd-be90-44b3-b2c5-9018255940b3][996fd2bd]] (the automatic half — a
gap containing an apply timestamp is provably human), and
[[id:44cef181-8dac-4735-8860-f123e132fdbc][44cef181]], which named this residue
and said explicitly that it stopped the line implying the minutes were captured
without capturing them.

*Measured in the accumulated record 2026-09-14, which this heading had argued
from mechanism alone.* Auditing the meta-work datetree and =Review attention=
for annotations with no matching =CLOCK:= line: **18 annotations assert a real
duration that nothing counts, totalling 99 minutes.** A further 10 have no CLOCK
line and are *zero-width*, which is correct by design and costs nothing.

The 18 range from 1 to 12 minutes and are overwhelmingly ordinary accepted spans
— 15 read =assigned at review=, two =root-cause unattributed spans, file
discipline fix=, one an authored note. They are spread across 2026-08-24 to
2026-09-10, so this is a steady leak rather than one bad pass.

*This is the first number attached to the defect, and it reframes the size.* The
body above establishes that a 0-run span applied as suggested writes an
annotation and no CLOCK line; this says what that has actually cost on **one**
heading family. The true total is larger: the audit covered only the meta-work
node and =Review attention=, not the hundred-odd task headings where the same
spans land.

*The record is not wrong, and that is the uncomfortable part.* Every one of
those 18 annotations is present, correct, and readable; a human scanning the
drawer sees the interval and its label. Only =org-clock-sum= and every
clocktable built on it skip them, because org totals CLOCK lines and annotations
are prose. So the drawer says the work happened and the report says it did not,
and neither is lying.

*One of the 18 is the case [[id:01849bef-fdc9-4c30-b467-00ca8606e8e0][01849bef]]
has been looking for* — =<2026-09-04 Fri 10:16>--<2026-09-04 Fri 10:21> read the
testdata report; planned the wrap-up sequence=, the user's own flip to active
notation. It is the sharpest illustration available: the interval was
*deliberately* marked as the human's attention, and still records zero minutes.


** TODO Guidepost aggregation ignores =cwd=, so two projects' turns cluster into one span :code:
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       c9940558-b23e-4b2c-86e8-e75a377634aa
:CREATED:  [2026-09-11 Fri 17:43]
:END:

=--aggregate-guideposts= clusters by time alone, so guideposts from concurrent
sessions in *different* projects merge into a single span — and accepting that
span credits one project's minutes to the other's heading. Found 2026-09-11 the
moment [[id:fbaf8009-b3dc-48ed-b099-63b74fc156a8][fbaf8009]]'s annotation made
the cwd visible: over the whole queue history exactly two groups render =[cwd:
agentic-euchre, claude-code-ide-org]=, and both are real —
=[2026-09-10 Thu 14:49:24]--[2026-09-10 Thu 15:02:17]=, already attributed to
=308bf4b4=, contains a 1:25 euchre run (14:59:33 resume, 15:00:58 pause), and
=[2026-09-10 Thu 15:59:24]--[2026-09-10 Thu 16:03:05]=, unassigned, opens with a
23s euchre run before three claude-code-ide-org guideposts. Small today because
euchre has 14 events against 722; it scales with the second project, and it is
silent. Proposed: treat a cwd change like the exclusions
=--aggregate-guideposts= already honours — a project boundary splits rather than
clusters through — which also makes each resulting span's annotation name one
tracker instead of two. This is the consumer half
[[id:5461c349-fd22-4b75-a508-50efbe4b87f8][5461c349]] recorded the field for;
note its other named consumer obligation, normalising a worktree's cwd to its
main checkout, or a single project splits into one span per worktree.


** NEXT A session id that changes mid-work splits one clock bracket across two queue files :code:
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       b57c7515-c511-4198-b311-26cefc52a60d
:CREATED:  [2026-09-11 Fri 18:17]
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
- State "NEXT"       from "TODO"       [2026-09-11 Fri 19:01] \\
  story had no next action; first in the new sequence
:END:

The queue is keyed by =session_id= and =--lane-clock-pairs= pairs within a lane
of =(session_id, agent_id)=, so a session whose id changes while work continues
leaves an unmatched =clock_in= in the old lane and an orphan =clock_out= in the
new one — neither pairs, and the bracket's attribution is lost in both
directions. Observed 2026-09-11: =f2e44fdf= holds =clock_in= on =325679af= at
17:48:30 and =b71c8eec= holds the matching =clock_out= at 18:14:33, with all of
that stretch's file-touching work in the second lane.
=bin/hooks/clock-target-check= fired correctly and could only report the
symptom, since from inside the new lane the earlier =clock_in= is invisible.
Note the two failure modes compose badly with =58332dd1=: the orphaned
=clock_in= is exactly the ever-widening span that heading describes, now
arriving through a second route. Worth measuring before designing — how often an
id changes mid-session, and whether the transcript's =sessionId= or a =leafUuid=
chain can bridge the two files retroactively, since the events themselves carry
enough to re-pair if something looked. The manual repair used this time was a
fresh matched pair in the new lane as an attribution anchor, leaving the
interval for a human to widen with =e=.

*Related, and deliberately not merged:*
[[id:9202b39d-49e4-441c-89f2-382feb4ef1ea][9202b39d]] filed the same root cause
on 2026-08-18 — one working stretch spanning two =session_id=s — for a different
event pair. That heading is about a **turn**, whose =resume= sits in the
launching session's file and whose =pause= lands in the job's; this is about a
**clock bracket**, whose =clock_in= and =clock_out= divide the same way.
Different pairing functions (=--span-events= adjacency against
=--lane-clock-pairs=), so two fixes, not one. Recording the link because two
headings on one cause with no reference between them is how a twin forms.

*And this case falsifies the generality of that heading's surviving rule, which
is the useful part.* What survived =9202b39d='s falsified depth counter is a
targeted linking rule: "a session whose stream **opens with a =pause=** is a
background job whose first turn began elsewhere". Measured here, =b71c8eec=
opens with a =resume= at 18:07:15 and carries its orphan =clock_out= at 18:14:33
— so the leading-=pause= test never fires, and the rule leaves this split
untouched. A second shape exists: a lane that opens normally and still holds
half of a bracket opened elsewhere.

*Which suggests the general statement is worth having before either fix is
built:* **any pairing keyed on =session_id= breaks when one stretch of work
spans two.** Two instances are now measured, on two different event pairs; the
question is whether the repair belongs per-pairing-function or once, at the
point where lanes are formed.

*The cause is upstream, established 2026-09-11 from the harness's own files, and
it means this condition is to be **tolerated**, not prevented.* Claude Code
re-keyed this session mid-work: =f2e44fdf='s transcript runs 17:24:27--17:55:49
and stops; =~/.claude/jobs/b71c8eec/= is created at **17:56:04** — 32 minutes
into an interactive session that until then had no job directory at all — and a
second job dir appears in the *same second*. The session then continues under
the job's own id, with its transcript re-stamped from the original 17:24:27
start, and =state.json= carrying =template: "bg"= and =respawnFlags=. An =API
Error: Connection lost mid-response= at 17:57:52 sits in that job's timeline
*after* the switch, so it is a consequence rather than the trigger.

*Known upstream, and reported less precisely than our own evidence states it.*
anthropics/claude-code
[[https://github.com/anthropics/claude-code/issues/59848][#59848]] ("Interactive
=claude= sessions are classified as background jobs post-2.1.139") describes
every session being *born* with =$CLAUDE_JOB_DIR= and =template: "bg"=; ours was
not — it ran half an hour without one, and the records appeared in a batch
mid-flight. That issue is **closed**, confirmed against 2.1.143, and its
headline symptom (the worktree-isolation guard) is indeed fixed here on 2.1.236,
while the classification it rested on is not.
[[https://github.com/anthropics/claude-code/issues/76220][#76220]] is the later
open report.

*So the design consequence is settled:* a session id is **not** a stable key for
a stretch of work, and nothing in this project can make it one. Any repair has
to reconcile lanes after the fact rather than assume the key holds — which is
the same conclusion the =9202b39d= link above reaches from the other direction.

*One consequence for [[id:96ddf1ef-67fd-4d3c-b8cc-01ebb14a687d][96ddf1ef]],
which is =NEXT= and will hit this immediately:* the re-key leaves **two
transcripts covering the same conversation**, the old one truncated at the
switch and the new one complete from the original start. A renderer keyed on
session id would render the same session twice under two ids, the first copy
silently short. It needs a rule for that before it renders anything.


* TODO Type a span's annotation at review time, not by hand-editing it after :code:
:PROPERTIES:
:CATEGORY: Apply
:ID:       8028e875-3032-4ade-949e-414d9861b928
:CREATED:  [2026-08-14 Fri 12:27]
:END:

*Observed 2026-08-14, in the file rather than in the abstract.* The user
hand-edited an applied annotation on
[[id:4f9e552a-e1a9-48dd-8801-3f23a31b9e32][4f9e552a]] after the fact,
rewriting =suggested span, accepted at review= to =task definition=. The
commit record confirms the correction was right — =d34c809= landed at
08:58, before the 09:09--09:42 span opened, so that interval really was
task definition and not the implementation it sat next to.

*The mechanism, read rather than assumed*
(=claude-code-ide-org--review-annotation-label=, =config.el= line 3298 as
of today). It returns one of three things:

- the enclosing =clock_in='s note, when the span inherited one — which is
  where a genuinely informative line like =show commits and :CREATED: in
  span windows= comes from;
- the empty string, for a bracketed span whose =clock_in= carried no note;
- =assigned at review= / =suggested span, accepted at review= for an
  *unbracketed* span, reconstructed from loose guideposts.

*The gap is the third case, and it is the case that matters.* An
unbracketed span is unbracketed precisely because no session ever said
what it was working on — so it is the one span with no note to inherit,
and the label it gets instead says only *that* it was reconstructed. Yet
at review the human has just decided what it was; that is what accepting
it means. The information exists, in someone's head, at exactly the
moment the line is written, and is discarded.

*A second finding, and it argues against the naive fix.* The hand-edit
*destroyed the provenance*. That boilerplate is not filler — the function's
docstring makes a deliberate case for it: provenance is "the one thing a
reader cannot recover from the timestamps," namely that the interval was
reconstructed and confirmed rather than clocked as it happened, which
matters for a record whose trustworthiness is a stated project goal.
Overwriting it in place trades one irrecoverable fact for another. They
are not competing, and a typed annotation should *append* to the
provenance rather than replace it — =task definition, accepted at review=
keeps both.

*Open, and worth settling before building:* whether the prompt belongs at
mark time (while the span's evidence lines are still on screen, which is
the argument for it) or at apply; whether it fires for every span or only
unbracketed ones; and whether an empty answer is allowed to fall through
to today's behaviour, which it probably must, since a review pass that
cannot be completed silently is worse than a vague label.

*Not blocking anything, and deliberately small.* It changes what one
function returns and adds one prompt. Related to
[[id:5ff5a4b8-2fee-472a-b5e1-c9de8cf96704][5ff5a4b8]] by adjacency — that
heading put the evidence on screen, and this one would capture the
conclusion the human draws from it — but neither requires the other.

- [2026-08-14 Fri 08:26]--[2026-08-14 Fri 08:28] suggested span, accepted at review

- [2026-08-14 Fri 08:37]--[2026-08-14 Fri 08:44] suggested span, accepted at review
CLOCK: [2026-08-14 Fri 08:49]--[2026-08-14 Fri 08:59] =>  0:10
- [2026-08-14 Fri 08:49]--[2026-08-14 Fri 08:59] suggested span, accepted at review
CLOCK: [2026-08-14 Fri 09:02]--[2026-08-14 Fri 09:04] =>  0:02
- [2026-08-14 Fri 09:02]--[2026-08-14 Fri 09:04] suggested span, accepted at review
:END:
:PLAN:

*Noticed 2026-08-13.* =WAIT= had quietly acquired a second, narrower
meaning here — the resting state at the end of a Claude work increment,
"the ball is in the human's court" — which is not what the =#+TODO:=
table says it means. The proposal was to read a =DOING -> WAIT=
transition as a *bracket*: a hard right edge after which guideposts
provably do not belong to that heading, killing the span guess's known
latching defect. It needed no new event kind — =todo= events already
carry state and timestamp whether or not anyone has applied them.

Two caveats came with it. The keyword is ambiguous: a =WAIT= meaning
"waiting on a vendor to email back" carries none of this, and the real
discriminator is probably the event's =source=/=session_id=. And the
bracket is only as reliable as its consistency — a session that ends
leaving a heading =DOING= emits nothing at all.

Composes with [[id:5ff5a4b8-2fee-472a-b5e1-c9de8cf96704][5ff5a4b8]]:
commits and =:CREATED:= stamps inside a window are the evidence,
brackets are the bounds on which windows are worth looking at. A
cheaper, mechanical partial answer to
[[id:0c8644ff-f7b1-4789-ac1e-babf2e9fbc8e][0c8644ff]], which proposes
asking Claude to narrate the same gaps; and it bounds the damage from
[[id:f4e628ce-e653-429a-8f3c-0f5d4241b139][f4e628ce]] and
[[id:09c134c4-31eb-4f6c-aa10-f836d1d28f16][09c134c4]], since a bracket
constrains what a stray guidepost can be attributed to even when the
guidepost itself is wrong.

*Provenance:* the pattern came from the user's own correction, not from
design — two instances, one person, one afternoon. An observed habit
worth testing, not a measured regularity.

:END:
*Twice overtaken by the code, and only one thing is left.*

*First, 2026-08-14.* The claim above that "nothing ever unsets it" was
wrong, and was already wrong when this heading was written.
=claude-code-ide-org--review-suggest-heading= already cleared the active
heading on every keyword except =DOING= and =PLANNING=, and had done
since =3c6b3f2= on 2026-08-13 — *hours before* this heading was
captured, with the latching-guess comment sitting directly above it.
Found by grep while inventorying keyword touch points; it was never
verified when the heading was written, which is the actual lesson.

*Second, and it closes one of the two survivors that correction left.*
The "denylist should be an allowlist" half shipped in =6a81822=
("Bracket the span guess on any keyword that is not work in progress").
The six named keywords are gone;
=claude-code-ide-org--work-in-progress-keywords= is =("DOING")= and
every other keyword brackets by construction — so adding =REVIEW=
needed no edit here, and neither did retiring =PLANNING=. Confirmed by
reading =config.el= on 2026-09-01.

*What is left is one thing, and it is data rather than logic.* The
bracket fires only when a =todo= event exists to fire it. A session that
ends leaving a heading =DOING= emits nothing, so the guess still latches
across exactly that case. Whether that is answered by a lint
([[id:3bd3402b-3ab6-4ace-b20b-3ff84caa3fb2][3bd3402b]]), a =Stop=-hook
transition, or a bracket inferred at review time is undecided.

The =WAIT= framing in the title is vestigial — the keyword was never the
load-bearing part — and the title should be rewritten if this is picked
up.


* TODO Re-measure the guidepost gap threshold against a month of queue data :code:research:
SCHEDULED: <2026-09-13 Sun +1m>
:PROPERTIES:
:CATEGORY: Queue
:ID:       96a51c2f-ce11-4b40-bdff-11223d3d4eb7
:CREATED:  [2026-08-13 Thu 12:20]
:END:

*Promoted out of [[id:b5f7c5c7-7ad6-4c68-9cce-3479db1f1644][b5f7c5c7]]
on 2026-08-13.* It was filed there because the threshold it re-measures
belongs to that epic's queue — but it carries a =+1m= repeater and so
never reaches =DONE=, which meant the epic could not either, and
[[id:38b92521-bba4-4bb5-9cd9-465862471a72][38b92521]] is blocked on that
epic. A recurring measurement has no terminal state and therefore no
completable parent.
:LOGBOOK:
- State "TODO"       from              [2026-08-13 Thu 12:21] \\
  scheduled re-measure, one month out
CLOCK: [2026-09-04 Fri 09:59]--[2026-09-04 Fri 10:00] =>  0:01
- [2026-09-04 Fri 09:59]--[2026-09-04 Fri 10:00] record what active marking would reverse
:END:

Scheduled rather than left to memory: the finding below is real but
thin, and the failure mode is adopting a four-day number as permanent
because nobody re-ran it.

*The survey, 2026-08-13.* 422 queue events, 2026-08-10 to 08-13, across
six sessions. Measured the *queue* rather than =:LOGBOOK:= deliberately:
=:LOGBOOK:= intervals are reconstructions produced by the very threshold
under test, so measuring them would partly measure the parameter.

Decomposed each session's stream into the two gaps that mean different
things:

- =resume= -> =pause= — how long the agent worked. n=111, median *81s*,
  p90 *211s*, one outlier over an hour (the concurrent subagent run).
- =pause= -> =resume= — how long the human took to reply. n=150, median
  *181s*.

*The result, and it is sharper than expected: the second is bimodal with
an empty band between the modes.* Sorted gaps run =… 949, 971, 1061=
and then jump straight to =2070, 2103, 2135 …=. *No gap anywhere in the
corpus falls between 1061s and 2070s.* A threshold sweep sees the same
hole from the other side: span count falls steeply, then sits flat at 29
across 1200-1800s, then resumes falling. Every value inside the band
produces an identical reconstruction.

Acted on immediately: =claude-code-ide-org-guidepost-gap-threshold= 900
-> *1200s*, the round number inside the band, at the start of the
plateau with margin either side. 900 sat just below the band and split
five spans nothing justifies splitting.

*A stated assumption the data contradicted, which is the more useful
half.* The survey was proposed on the premise that silence from ~18:00
to ~08:00 is almost certainly AFK. True, but not where the long gaps
are: of 19 gaps over 2000s, *11 start inside working hours* — 07:00,
10:00, 12:00, 14:00, 15:00, 16:00, 17:00 — including the two longest
(151 and 158 minutes, starting 12:59 and 15:34). Daytime absence is
indistinguishable from nighttime absence. Gap *length* separates the
modes cleanly; clock hour does not. Recorded against
[[id:7771fc63-9374-4c8a-9e80-24ceec21977c][7771fc63]], whose
working-hours guess rests on exactly the premise this refutes.

*What to re-run on the scheduled date*, and the reason the number is not
yet settled: n=150 gaps, four days, one person, during an unusually
intense stretch of work on this very project. The band may be an artifact
of that. Re-derive the histogram and the sweep; if the band has moved or
closed, retune again and say so here rather than defending 1200.

*This is a moving target, not a one-off calibration* — the user's own
framing, 2026-08-13, and it changes what the re-measure is for.
Proficiency, setup and work objectives all evolve, and the habits
generating these gaps evolve with them. A faster operator, a different
mix of long-running subagent work, or a stretch of shallow triage rather
than deep implementation would each shift the modes and could close the
band entirely. So the finding above describes *August 2026*, not this
project. The scheduled task is not "confirm 1200 was right"; it is
"re-derive, and expect a different answer."

*Made a =+1m= repeater at the user's instruction, 2026-08-13*, which is
the honest encoding of "moving target": a one-shot date would have
implied the number gets settled once. It does not. Each occurrence
re-derives the histogram and the sweep against whatever the preceding
month's habits actually were, and either confirms the current value or
moves it.

Practical note for whoever handles an occurrence: marking it =DONE=
rolls the =SCHEDULED= stamp forward a month and returns the heading to
=TODO= — org's own repeater behavior — so the record of *that* month's
finding has to be written into this body before closing it, or it is
gone. Append; do not overwrite the previous month's numbers.

Charts from the original survey are an artifact, not committed here —
regenerate rather than trusting a stale rendering.

*Retarget before the September run — this is now pointed at the wrong
knob.* Since 2026-08-18 (=226ed53b= step 2(b)) the gap threshold governs
only *grouping and display*: it decides how many items a review buffer
shows and how wide each reads, and moves no recorded minute. Its
docstring says so. Re-measuring it on a month of data would answer a
question whose answer changes nothing.

What now decides recorded duration is the pair of floors:

- =claude-code-ide-org-span-idle-floor= (120s) — how much idle is
  absorbed into a run.
- =claude-code-ide-org-span-minimum-interval= — the floor on what gets
  written, named 2026-08-20 by
  [[id:e0c55d26-706c-42b6-9405-54e9d5ed4750][e0c55d26]]. It is 0 today,
  meaning the two rendering conditions alone; its docstring carries the
  sweep this review needs. *Do not cite a stored number.* 120s measured
  at 6.5% of recorded time on 2026-08-18 and at 3.03% two days later on
  a grown corpus — the estimate halved without the code changing.

Both rest on eight days of data. This heading's own body already names
that failure: /"adopting a four-day number as permanent because nobody
re-ran it."/ Same trap, one knob over.

Keep the =+1m= repeater and the parentless placement — the reasoning
recorded above still holds, and applies to the floors identically.

*Interim observation 2026-09-01, twelve days before the scheduled occurrence and
deliberately not one.* Prompted by the user asking whether the 1200s figure had
drifted. It has, and the headline is the one this body said to look for.

*The empty band has closed.* The August survey's central finding was that no gap
anywhere in the corpus fell between *1061s and 2070s* — a hole that made every
value inside it produce an identical reconstruction, which is what justified
picking 1200. Against the corpus as it stands there are *43 gaps inside that
band*.

*The corpus is now large enough that this is not sampling noise.*

| | Aug 2026 survey | 2026-09-01 |
|--------------------------+-----------------+------------|
| distinct days | 4 | *22* (2026-08-07 .. 09-01) |
| queue events | 422 | *2735* |
| =pause= -> =resume= gaps | 150 | *867* |
| =resume= -> =pause= runs | 111 | *886* |
| gap median | 181s | *232s* |

So the band was an artifact of four days, exactly as this body allowed for —
/"n=150 gaps, four days, one person, during an unusually intense stretch of work
on this very project. The band may be an artifact of that."/ It was. The
prediction and the refutation are both recorded here, which is the value of
having written the caveat down.

*What this does and does not change.* 1200s is no longer a round number inside a
hole; it is an arbitrary point on a continuum. But since =226ed53b= the gap
threshold governs only *grouping and display*, so an arbitrary value there costs
legibility rather than accuracy — which is why the retarget note above still
stands and the floors remain the live question.

*Explicitly not the scheduled run.* This is a histogram check on one statistic.
It is not the sweep: no span-count-versus-threshold curve, no re-derivation for
=span-idle-floor=, no percentage-of-recorded-time figure for the floors. The
occurrence on =<2026-09-13 Sun>= should still do all of that, and should now
expect to *move* 1200 rather than confirm it.

*A third question the September run should absorb, from the user's framing
2026-09-01.* The floors are tuned to keep the human's idle *out* of the agent's
clock, which is right for per-heading attribution and backwards for the ratio of
human time to agent time that the user is actually curious about
([[id:20cecd53-2338-4237-ab06-f8129e10a2c8][20cecd53]]). Measured on one session
that day: of ~90 minutes of plausible at-keyboard gap time, 3 minutes were
credited to the agent and 87 recorded nowhere. The same histogram answers both
questions; only the threshold's *purpose* differs. Re-deriving without asking
what the gaps say about the human would waste the pass.

*A second question for the September occurrence, added by the user 2026-09-04.*
Alongside re-deriving the threshold: **assess whether a clear class of spans
emerges that should be marked _active_ — angle-bracketed — at review time**,
meaning the interval was the human's attention rather than the agent's.

That is the automatic half of =01849bef=, which today gives the review buffer no
key for the flip and leaves it as four bracket characters edited by hand. The
distinction is not cosmetic: =b8e6007a= settled that the queue records *agent*
activity and the agenda must not absorb it, so an active timestamp is an
assertion about *whose* time an interval was, and it is the only kind the agenda
should show.

**Do not expect the class to be found by a rule.** =409a69ff= now records the
user's own account of inconsistent =AFK= discipline — popping in mid-turn to
queue a prompt, then out again without re-declaring — which makes a marker land
in the middle of an absence as often as at its edge. So the question this
occurrence answers is empirical and narrower than "what is the heuristic":
*given a month of spans, is there a subset whose evidence is unambiguous enough
to mark without asking?* A negative answer is a real result and should be
recorded as one, since it retires the search rather than leaving it open
indefinitely.

The corpus for it is already larger than the threshold work needs: the queue,
the transcripts, and the =AFK= interjections that =e75d660f= says are ground
truth and are currently discarded.

*Checked 2026-09-04, prompted by the user asking what a no-run span actually
writes — and the answer changes what "mark it active" costs.*

**A span with no runs is not a no-op.** =--review-apply-clock= writes no CLOCK
line for it, and does write a =:LOGBOOK:= annotation, gated on =(unless
observed)=. Its docstring gives the reason: "the guideposts are real evidence
that something happened, and the absent CLOCK line is the same refusal to claim
a duration nobody observed." So the evidence is kept and the duration is
deliberately not claimed. (An earlier statement in conversation that such a span
"records nothing" was wrong.)

**Which means the active-marking question is a policy reversal, not a formatting
change**, and it must answer the measurement that produced the current policy
rather than pass it by. =--review-format-annotation= says "Always *inactive*
timestamps, so nothing written from the queue reaches =org-agenda=", and it says
so because an active branch **existed and was removed**: it branched on
=:agent=, testing "is this a subagent" while claiming to test "is this a human",
and on 2026-08-14 all 68 events of one session carried =agent_id= nil — so every
span it produced was published to the agenda as though the user had been at the
keyboard. Its conclusion is the sentence this proposal contradicts: *"there is
no case in which a queue-derived span is the user's own attention, and no branch
to make."*

*The mechanism is not the obstacle.* =c084553c= established that an active
timestamp inside =:LOGBOOK:= does reach the agenda, and the docstring confirms
that remains the mechanism — "reserved for intervals a human logs themselves".
So the edit is small; the argument is the expensive part.

*The distinction that keeps =01849bef= clear of this.* A **keystroke** is a
human logging their own interval, which the current design already sanctions —
=4f8500e6= notes a human clocking in Emacs writes a bare CLOCK line with no
annotation at all. Only the **automatic** half asks the queue to produce
something the design says only a human may produce. So the manual half can ship
without reopening any of this; this occurrence decides the other half.

*What the September pass therefore has to establish*, and it is a higher bar
than "is there a pattern": evidence that distinguishes the human's attention
**without inferring it from a field that means something else**, which is
exactly how the removed branch failed. =AFK= markers, an apply timestamp inside
the gap (=996fd2bd=) and the transcript are the candidates; =409a69ff= already
records why the first of those is fuzzier than it looks.

*Correction to the note above, same day.* It said the active branch in
=--review-format-annotation= "existed and was removed", and quoted that
function's own conclusion — "there is no case in which a queue-derived span is
the user's own attention, and no branch to make".

**That quote is from a stale docstring.** The function's =let*= four lines below
it reads =(fmt (if (plist-get item :active) "<%Y-%m-%d %a %H:%M>" "[...]"))=.
The branch was *re-added* on 2026-08-24 — keyed on a human's typed bracket style
through =e=, not on =:agent=, which is the distinction the removal argument
actually turned on.

So the removal argument stands and is narrower than the docstring now implies:
what was removed is *inferring* human-ness from a queue field. What exists is a
human *asserting* it. This occurrence still has to answer the first question —
whether evidence can distinguish attention without inferring it from a field
meaning something else — but it is no longer proposing to reinstate something
the project decided against. Half of it is already built.

*Recorded as a lesson rather than only a correction:* the stale sentence misled
a reading of that function **twice in one day**, in opposite directions, and
both times the code four lines away said otherwise. Filed separately.
