Proposed by the user 2026-08-10: a standing pseudo-task to clock against
for grooming, capturing, "what's next" review, and similar activity that
is real work but belongs to no single tracked task. Captured as TODO on
a recommendation, not as a settled decision.

*The concrete argument, from that session's own data.* Of the first ten
events in its queue, *eight* were =pause=/=resume= guideposts that
reduced to nothing at review. Guideposts carry no heading; they inherit
whichever one the surrounding =clock_in=/=clock_out= last named, so
guideposts recorded while no clock is open are unattributable and
silently dropped. Meta-work is precisely when no clock is open — so that
time is not merely misattributed, it is invisible, in direct tension
with this project's second stated goal.

*Check org's own mechanism first, per
[[id:c084553c-0621-4a96-9fa1-f32850aeec6a][c084553c]].* Org already has
a name for the concept: =org-clock-default-task=, set with
=org-clock-mark-default-task=.

*Two risks were recorded, neither disqualifying.* The *dumping-ground*
failure — a large bucket labelled "misc" answers no question worth
asking, so it must be scoped to genuinely cross-cutting work. And it
*never reaches DONE*: a permanently open task sits among siblings
governed by the single-=NEXT= triggers, and it never archives, so its
=:LOGBOOK:= grows without bound. Probably fine for a pure time record,
but a decision rather than an oversight.

*Refined 2026-08-10, and the refinement narrows what this is for.* The
user proposed the general principle: grooming, defining, amending and
adjusting a future task is *activity on that task*, and should be
clocked against it, leaving its TODO state untouched. Never stated
anywhere, though nothing forbade it — CLAUDE.md's rules say a transition
*to* =DOING= opens a clock and one *from* it closes one, never the
converse, so clocking a =TODO= heading was always legal by the letter
and simply undescribed. Confirmed safe the same day:
=org-clock-in-switch-to-state= is nil here, and this heading's own
editorial session was clocked against
[[id:02aaae22-0958-4c0f-b5bb-2cbea32416ae][02aaae22]] while that heading
stayed literally =TODO=.

Attribution is then exact where it applies — time spent working out what
a task should be lands on that task, next to the reasoning it produced.
A catch-all could answer "how much meta-work happened" but never "which
task was I circling." *So the two are complementary and this heading
shrinks to the residue:* work belonging to no single task — backlog-wide
"what's next?" surveys, cross-cutting review, triage before a task
exists to attach to. Smaller than the original framing assumed, which
also blunts the dumping-ground risk: anything reaching the catch-all is
there because it truly has no home.

*Consequence accepted deliberately:* a =TODO= heading can accrue real
clock time without ever having been =DOING=. Correct — deciding what a
task is *is* work — but "time spent" and "was worked on" stop being the
same question, and any future reporting must keep them apart.

*Alternative captured 2026-08-11, narrowing this further:*
[[id:3d0487f4-b8f8-451c-88bf-ab537663cbd0][3d0487f4]] proposes that the
review buffer surface unattributed guideposts for retrospective
assignment, rather than requiring anyone to clock into a standing task
in advance. If that lands, this stops being a mechanism to remember and
becomes a convenient assignment target.

:END:
*Decided 2026-08-21, in conversation with the user, and scoped into five
children.*

The standing pseudo-task becomes a *datetree*: one node per day under a
=Review and planning= category, clocked against directly rather than
captured beneath.

*The day node answers this heading's own unbounded-=:LOGBOOK:= risk* by
construction — it is bounded, and the year rolls into DONE.org on the
existing =:ARCHIVE:= route, so the unbounded case never arises.

*The dumping-ground risk gets one concrete guard:* the day node carries
*no TODO keyword*, so =claude-code-ide-org--review-suggest-heading= will
never propose it, and after
[[id:62c6b1be-4e3c-4ea1-9f0c-19ee70be0e1e][62c6b1be]] it would refuse a
grouping anyway. Every span landing there costs a deliberate =a= at
review: a place time is *assigned* to, not one it *falls* into.

*Creation is on demand, not scheduled.* The user asked whether a cron
job or a recurrence should create the node; measured against three
consequences — empty nodes on non-working days, a stored id that can go
stale, and a scheduler failure that announces nothing — the answer was
neither. See [[id:9575e65b-b589-465a-830c-286541780f96][9575e65b]]. The
daily =SCHEDULED: <... +1d>= repeater is kept for the *ritual*: it
reminds, it does not create.

*One draft was falsified along the way*
([[id:25ec37b0-460f-404d-be83-8a4b4d355cdc][25ec37b0]]): the outline
path in =file+olp+datetree= does not nest the tree — nesting comes from
a =:DATE_TREE:= property. Worth knowing before writing the capture
template from the template name alone, which is how the first draft went
wrong.

*Correction [2026-08-27 Thu]: a paragraph claiming =org-clock-default-task= was
"adopted … now wired rather than merely noted" was wrong,* and contradicted its
own next sentence, which said Claude cannot use it at all because every clock
call goes through =org_clock_in= with an =:ID:=.
[[id:6ae83993-906c-4d8b-b11e-c91be8996962][6ae83993]] states the fact: *zero
references to =clock-default-task= across =modules/= and the Doom config.* It
has never been wired.

*What actually shipped is ours, not org's:*
=claude-code-ide-org--ensure-day-node= plus =org_clock_in= accepting the
*category title* in place of an =:ID:=, resolving today's node on demand and
filing the interval against it. That is why allocation works today and has for
days — the user noticed exactly this and asked.

*So the check-org-first item was closed against the wrong evidence.* Org did
have a name for the concept, which is what the item asked. It does not follow
that we used it, and the body recorded the first as though it settled the
second. Being right about the search and wrong about the outcome is the more
dangerous shape, because it reads as diligence.

*What =org-clock-default-task= would still buy, narrowly:* the *human*, in
Emacs, clocking meta-work with =C-u C-u C-c C-x C-i= without navigating to
today's node. Nothing for Claude, nothing for the queue. 6ae83993 stays open on
those terms. And the interval it would produce is inactive, necessarily —
measured 2026-08-27, =org-clock-in= writes =CLOCK: [ts]--[ts]= and org has no
active variant of a CLOCK line at all; the distinction this project cares about
lives on the *annotation* line beneath, so what marks such an interval as the
human's own is the annotation's *absence*
([[id:4f8500e6-b468-4fd4-a0f6-ebf04c7a57ba][4f8500e6]]).

*Two further corrections, 2026-09-01, both found by checking rather than
recalling.*

- The never-reaches-DONE risk above named =--trigger-auto-promote-sole-todo= as
  one of the triggers it would sit among. That trigger was *retired* — 163 lines
  removed, along with its three guards — and only test comments still mention
  it. =--trigger-demote-conflicting-next= survives and is the only one left that
  a permanently open sibling could interact with.
- *The rule this heading asked for was never written.* It called for a line in
  CLAUDE.md under "State transition rules" stating that a clock may run on a
  heading in any state, and that grooming attaches to the task being groomed.
  Neither CLAUDE.md nor =.claude/rules/org-conventions.md= says so today. The
  convention is in force and undocumented, which is the condition this heading
  was raised to end.


** TODO Teach org_capture to target a datetree, with an addressable day node :code:
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       25ec37b0-460f-404d-be83-8a4b4d355cdc
:CREATED:  [2026-08-21 Fri 09:51]
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
- State "TODO"       from              [2026-08-21 Fri 09:52] \\
  datetree target and addressable day node
- State "NEXT"       from "TODO"       [2026-08-21 Fri 10:27] \\
  Auto-promoted: sole remaining TODO in its sibling group.
- State "TODO"       from "NEXT"       [2026-08-21 Fri 10:47] \\
  Auto-demoted: superseded by sibling [[id:e30d52d7-960b-44f3-ab92-93846c81a1bd][bin/lint-org will drown in datetree scaffolding it can never satisfy]] becoming NEXT.
:END:

=claude-code-ide-org--capture-target-spec= resolves exactly three shapes: =(file
F)=, =(id ID)= and =(file+olp F CATEGORY)=. A datetree needs a fourth.

*The olp in =file+olp+datetree= does not nest the tree, which is the opposite of
what the template's own name suggests.* Read from org-capture.el rather than
assumed: the branch resolves the outline path to a marker, goes to it -- and
then calls =(widen)= and hands =org-datetree-find-date-create= the date with
*no* =keep-restriction= argument. The marker is discarded. Nesting comes from
somewhere else entirely.

*Two mechanisms actually control placement*, both in
=org-datetree--find-create-group=:

- =keep-restriction= = ='subtree-at-point=, which narrows to the heading at
  point and sets =org-datetree-base-level= from it. Capture never passes this.
- a =:DATE_TREE:= property, found anywhere in the file by =org-find-property=
  after the widen. When present, org narrows to that heading and sets the base
  level from it. *This is the one capture actually uses.*

*Materialized both ways rather than reasoned about.* Without the property, two
captures against =* Review and planning= produced two separate =* 2026= trees at
*level 1*, outside the category and duplicated. With =:DATE_TREE: t= on the
category, one tree nested correctly:

#+BEGIN_EXAMPLE
,* Review and planning
:PROPERTIES:
:DATE_TREE: t
:ARCHIVE:  DONE.org::* Review and planning
:END:
,** 2026
,*** 2026-08 August
,**** 2026-08-19 Wednesday
,**** 2026-08-21 Friday
#+END_EXAMPLE

*=:DATE_TREE:= is permitted on a category by the narrowed rule, not in spite of
it.* =.claude/rules/org-conventions.md= allows "a *structural* property, which
says something about the grouping rather than about work", and names =:ARCHIVE:=
as the case that forced the wording. =:DATE_TREE:= is the same kind of statement
-- it says how this grouping is organised -- and sits beside =:ARCHIVE:= on
identical footing.

*The day node is bare, and that is the part that blocks everything else.* Org
writes =**** 2026-08-21 Friday= with no property drawer at all. The whole point
of this design is to *clock against the day node*, and every tool here addresses
headings by =:ID:=, so the capture path must stamp =:ID:= and =:CREATED:= on the
day node at creation. Without that the node exists and is unreachable.

*Verified on org 9.6.15* (the batch Emacs =bin/test= runs). The live Emacs is on
*9.8.7*; both carry =org-datetree--find-create-group= and the =:DATE_TREE:=
lookup, but the split is [[id:1ed7b2b4-a809-4547-b04f-099a3111fc1e][1ed7b2b4]]'s
undeclared-dependency gap showing up in practice, and a datetree test will run
against the older one.


** MAYBE Point org-clock-default-task at the day node, and re-point it daily :code:
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       6ae83993-906c-4d8b-b11e-c91be8996962
:CREATED:  [2026-08-21 Fri 09:51]
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
- State "TODO"       from              [2026-08-21 Fri 09:52] \\
  wire and re-point the default task
- State "MAYBE"      from "TODO"       [2026-08-28 Fri 07:55] \\
  buys only human convenience now
:END:

=org-clock-default-task= is org's own answer to "somewhere to clock
cross-cutting time", which is why
[[id:a1d63f52-4c07-4b9e-8f31-6d2b90c4e7a5][a1d63f52]] pointed at it under
c084553c's check-org-first rule. Chosen by the user 2026-08-21, alongside the
datetree.

*It is entirely unwired today.* Zero references to =clock-default-task= across
=modules/= and =~/.config/doom/config.el=. Nothing to migrate; this is a first
wiring.

*It moves every day, and nothing moves it.* The variable holds a *marker*, set
from point by =org-clock-mark-default-task=. With a datetree the intended target
is a different heading each morning, so on any day after the first the default
task points at yesterday. Something has to re-point it -- a =SessionStart= hook,
an idle timer, or on-demand resolution when the day node is created. Deciding
which is the substance of this heading; the wiring itself is one =setq=.

*It serves the human and not Claude, and that asymmetry should be accepted
deliberately rather than discovered.* =org-clock-default-task= is a live-Emacs
convenience: clock into it with a keystroke. Claude cannot use it at all --
every clock call here goes through =org_clock_in= with an =:ID:=, and the queue
architecture means Claude never touches a live clock in the first place. So this
buys *you* one-keystroke meta-work clocking, while Claude still needs the day
node resolved to a UUID.

*Do not solve Claude's half here.* Resolving "today's node" to an =:ID:= without
pasting a UUID is [[id:915378ac-3bd4-461d-b0f0-83f67fbb3dac][915378ac]]'s
subject, under [[id:478d6ec9-1b99-4c6c-88e9-c793ad4b2f22][478d6ec9]]. Building a
second lookup here would be the parallel-convention mistake c084553c exists to
prevent, one level down.


** DONE bin/lint-org will drown in datetree scaffolding it can never satisfy :code:
CLOSED: [2026-08-21 Fri 13:20]
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       e30d52d7-960b-44f3-ab92-93846c81a1bd
:CREATED:  [2026-08-21 Fri 09:51]
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
- State "TODO"       from              [2026-08-21 Fri 09:52] \\
  exempt datetree scaffolding from CREATED
- State "NEXT"       from "TODO"       [2026-08-21 Fri 10:32] \\
  must land before the tree exists
CLOCK: [2026-08-21 Fri 11:59]--[2026-08-21 Fri 12:00] =>  0:01
- [2026-08-21 Fri 11:59]--[2026-08-21 Fri 12:00] measure the real lint scope
CLOCK: [2026-08-21 Fri 13:16]--[2026-08-21 Fri 13:20] =>  0:04
- State "DOING"      from "NEXT"       [2026-08-21 Fri 13:16] \\
  revise the level rule for the datetree
- [2026-08-21 Fri 13:16]--[2026-08-21 Fri 13:20] teach the lint about datetree scaffolding
- State "DONE"       from "DOING"      [2026-08-21 Fri 13:20] \\
  level rule scoped, four mutations verified
:END:
:PLAN:

=bin/lint-org= warns on any heading missing =:CREATED:=. Datetree scaffolding
can never satisfy it: org writes =** 2026=, =*** 2026-08 August= and =****
2026-08-21 Friday= bare, and a year node has no creation moment worth stamping
anyway.

*The arithmetic is what makes this a blocker rather than a nuisance.* One day
node per working day plus a month node each month is on the order of 300-400
headings a year, against *18 warnings in the whole repo today*. The scaffolding
would outnumber the real findings roughly twenty to one inside the first year,
and a warning list nobody reads is the same as no lint at all -- the failure
mode [[id:e0904e93-b99d-44ad-b641-e3e13aced8c4][e0904e93]] just finished fixing
in the session-start report, arriving in a second place.

*Fix it before the tree exists, not after.* Once several hundred warnings are
already there the temptation is to silence the check rather than exempt the
class, and those are very different decisions.

*Exempt the class, not the file.* The day node is the one that will carry =:ID:=
and =:CREATED:= (see [[id:25ec37b0-460f-404d-be83-8a4b4d355cdc][25ec37b0]]), so
it should stay linted like any other tracked heading; only the *year and month*
nodes are pure structure. That distinction is mechanical -- a datetree year or
month node is a heading whose title matches org's own datetree format and which
sits under a =:DATE_TREE:= heading -- so the exemption can be stated precisely
rather than as "skip anything that looks like a date".

*Note this is the same shape as the categories exemption already in the linter*,
which skips =:ID:=/=:CREATED:= on top-level headings because they are structure
rather than work. Worth reading that code first; this may be one more case for
an existing predicate rather than a new branch.

*Measured 2026-08-21 by linting the exact proposed structure, and the scope
above is understated.* The warning flood is the least of it — the real blockers
are three *errors*, which the pre-commit hook refuses on:

#+BEGIN_EXAMPLE
error: heading has no :ID:: 2026
error: heading has no :ID:: 2026-08 August
error: level-4 heading; the file has three levels (category, task, epic-child): 2026-08-21 Friday
warn : heading has no :CREATED:: 2026
warn : heading has no :CREATED:: 2026-08 August
#+END_EXAMPLE

Two corrections follow. The year and month nodes fail on *=:ID:=*, an error,
before they ever reach the =:CREATED:= warning this heading was written about.
And the *level-4 rule rejects the day node itself* — the one heading that does
carry =:ID:= and =:CREATED:=, and the one we most want linted. Exempting
scaffolding does not reach it.

*The level rule is an assertion with a source*, recorded on
[[id:3bd3402b-3ab6-4ace-b20b-3ff84caa3fb2][3bd3402b]] and restated in
=bin/lint-org='s header as "the file has three levels (category, task,
epic-child)". A datetree makes that false: category, year, month, day is four
before any task exists. So this is not a lint tweak but a revision of a
documented structural claim about TODO.org, and 3bd3402b should be read before
the rule is touched.

*Consequence for sequencing:* proceeding as previously scoped would exempt the
two warnings, then hit three errors at
[[id:cd1e974e-e006-4974-b770-cdc1cf2e4ab2][cd1e974e]] with the category
half-written.

:END:
*Shipped 2026-08-21, before the tree exists.* All three errors are gone and none
of them was silenced. =claude-code-ide-org--lint-file= now tracks the innermost
enclosing =:DATE_TREE:= heading's level while it maps entries --
=org-map-entries= walks in document order, so an anchor is always seen before
its descendants -- and classifies each descendant by a new
=claude-code-ide-org--datetree-node-role=.

*The level rule is scoped, not dropped.* 3bd3402b's claim -- 624 headings, zero
at level 4 -- was true of a file with no datetree in it. It now reads: three
levels outside a =:DATE_TREE:= subtree, and inside one exactly org's year/month
scaffolding plus its day node. A level-4 heading anywhere else is still an
error, and so is anything *below* a day node, since nothing is filed under one.
=bin/lint-org='s header carries the same revised wording;
=.claude/rules/org-conventions.md= never stated the rule, so nothing there
needed changing.

*Gated on org's literal title shapes, not on depth*, and that is the
load-bearing choice. A =:DATE_TREE:= category holds real tasks beside its tree
-- [[id:cd1e974e-e006-4974-b770-cdc1cf2e4ab2][cd1e974e]]'s ritual repeater is a
level-2 heading under that very category, exactly where the year node sits -- so
a depth-only exemption would have waived =:ID:= and =:CREATED:= for it and for
anything ever refiled there. That is the same over-application shape guarded
twice in [[id:42808717-2736-4bf4-872e-887f990203ca][42808717]] and
[[id:c8a6c5d2-8777-44d2-9900-e56eb03aa2d9][c8a6c5d2]] earlier the same day: a
predicate reading position without asking what kind of heading it is looking at.
The patterns are org's own =comparefun= regexes, read out of the straight
checkout's =org-datetree.el= rather than recalled. Quarter and ISO-week
groupings are deliberately not accepted.

*The day node stays linted, which took an explicit fall-through.* Writing the
level clause as a branch that matched the day node and did nothing would have
exempted the one heading here that most needs =:ID:= -- it is what this project
clocks against. The condition is =and=-ed instead, so a day node falls through
to the ordinary =:ID:=/=:CREATED:= checks.

*Verified by mutation, four ways, each failing exactly its own test:* removing
the year/month exemption, refusing the day node its level-4 allowance, making
the role depth-only, and treating the day node as scaffolding. The third is the
one that matters -- it is the only mutation the depth-only design would have
shipped. Fixtures are literal text and never call
=org-datetree-find-date-create=, keeping
[[id:1ed7b2b4-a809-4547-b04f-099a3111fc1e][1ed7b2b4]]'s 9.6-vs-9.8 org split out
of a lint that only reads structure. =bin/lint-org= on the real files: 0 errors,
23 pre-existing warnings.

*A second datetree is already visible, and this rule does not cover it.*
Measured 2026-08-21, hours after shipping: if DONE.org ever takes an
=org-archive-location= of the =datetree/= form, org builds a year/month/day tree
there *with no =:DATE_TREE:= property on it* and files entries at *level 4*
beneath the day node. Against the rule as written that is four errors per
archived item -- three scaffolding nodes with no =:ID:=, plus a level-4 heading
that is not a day node.

Not a defect in what shipped: the rule was scoped to the structure that existed,
and it correctly refuses a shape nobody has adopted. But it means the exemption
is keyed on a property org's own archive path never writes, so "recognise a
datetree" and "recognise our =:DATE_TREE:= anchor" are not the same predicate.
Whoever takes up [[id:29439196-bbb8-4b64-b8d5-3bf9d457bf6c][29439196]] or the
archive-cadence question should read this rule before choosing an archive
location, not after.


** DONE Resolve today's node on demand, never storing or scheduling its ID :code:
CLOSED: [2026-08-24 Mon 12:31]
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       9575e65b-b589-465a-830c-286541780f96
:CREATED:  [2026-08-21 Fri 10:10]
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
- State "TODO"       from              [2026-08-21 Fri 10:11] \\
  build the on-demand resolver
- State "NEXT"       from "TODO"       [2026-08-21 Fri 13:22] \\
  nominated: resolver gates both remaining siblings
CLOCK: [2026-08-24 Mon 12:27]--[2026-08-24 Mon 12:31] =>  0:04
- State "DOING"      from "NEXT"       [2026-08-24 Mon 12:27] \\
  build the on-demand day-node resolver
- [2026-08-24 Mon 12:27]--[2026-08-24 Mon 12:31] build the day-node resolver
- State "DONE"       from "DOING"      [2026-08-24 Mon 12:31] \\
  resolver shipped, dated from the event
CLOCK: [2026-08-24 Mon 12:46]--[2026-08-24 Mon 12:48] =>  0:02
- [2026-08-24 Mon 12:46]--[2026-08-24 Mon 12:48] make the category assignable at review
CLOCK: [2026-08-24 Mon 13:02]--[2026-08-24 Mon 13:03] =>  0:01
- [2026-08-24 Mon 13:02]--[2026-08-24 Mon 13:03] make the category assignable at review
:END:
:PLAN:

One function answers "which heading is today's meta-work node", and it is called
at the moment something needs the answer rather than run ahead of time. Steps:
find the =:DATE_TREE:= heading; =org-datetree-find-date-create= for today, which
is org's own idempotent find-or-create; stamp =:ID:= and =:CREATED:= if the node
was just made; set =org-clock-default-task= to its marker; return the id.

*Two callers, and the asymmetry between them is the design.* =org_clock_in=
calls it when the target names the category, and *creates* if absent. The
=SessionStart= hook calls it to refresh =org-clock-default-task= and must
*never* create -- a session starting is not evidence that meta-work happened.

*Why on demand rather than scheduled, which was the question that produced this
heading.* The user asked 2026-08-21 whether creation should be a cron job or a
recurrence. Three consequences decided it:

- *A schedule manufactures empty nodes.* One per calendar day whether or not
  anything happened -- on the order of 365 a year against a working year's worth
  of real ones. That is [[id:e30d52d7-960b-44f3-ab92-93846c81a1bd][e30d52d7]]'s
  scaffolding problem being fed rather than contained.
- *A stored id goes stale; a computed one cannot.* The premise of the original
  question -- "the default id would have to be updated to match today's node" --
  is only true if something remembers yesterday's. Recomputing deletes the
  failure mode instead of scheduling around it.
- *Nothing announces a scheduler that stopped running.* A missing node is
  noticed the moment someone clocks; a silent scheduler failure is noticed at
  review, days later, as absent time.

*The scheduling tools available to Claude cannot do this job anyway.*
=CronCreate= and the =schedule= skill run *cloud* agents, which have no route to
this machine's Emacs server. Recorded from the tooling description rather than
from a test, but the shape is not marginal. A local scheduler would also fire on
non-working days, and -- recursively -- a session spawned to fix attribution
generates its own guideposts needing attribution.

*Claude never learns the daily UUID, which is the point.* The target is the
stable category title =Review and planning=, reusing the precedent
=claude-code-ide-org--capture-target-spec= already argues for: top-level
categories are few, human-curated, and never move, so the properties that make
titles unsafe elsewhere do not hold. That sidesteps
[[id:915378ac-3bd4-461d-b0f0-83f67fbb3dac][915378ac]] rather than waiting on it.

*Shared by [[id:25ec37b0-460f-404d-be83-8a4b4d355cdc][25ec37b0]] and
[[id:6ae83993-906c-4d8b-b11e-c91be8996962][6ae83993]]*, which each need part of
it -- the day node's =:ID:= and the default-task marker respectively. Build it
once here rather than twice there.

*Open design question, recorded 2026-08-21 (user-flagged for their decision,
found reviewing the next-session prompt).* "Two callers: =org_clock_in= creates"
is unsettled, not settled: =org_clock_in= is a queued tool that by architecture
changes nothing when called. Either (a) the resolver runs at *queue time* -- an
immediate file write triggered by a queued tool, a new hybrid class like
=org_amend=, so the day-node =:ID:= is resolved while "today" is still today; or
(b) it runs at *apply time* inside the review command -- pure, but an event
queued late Monday and applied Tuesday resolves "today" to the wrong day's node.
(a) keeps the timestamp honest and (b) keeps the tool pure; they cannot both
hold. Decide before building; the next-session prompt no longer implies this is
specified.

*The open question above has three answers, not the two it poses.* Recorded
2026-08-21 at the user's request, so the analysis does not have to be rebuilt;
the decision itself is still theirs.

*(a) Queue time -- =org_clock_in= creates the node when called.* Resolves
"today" while today is still today, and hands Claude the id inside the session.
Against: it breaks the invariant that queued tools change nothing, which was
bought with a documented run of desync bugs; a background or concurrent session
would write to TODO.org unreviewed; and a node is created even for a clock event
the human later dismisses, which is precisely the empty-node problem this
heading's on-demand design exists to prevent.

*(b) Apply time, dated "today" -- the review command creates it.* Tools stay
pure, every write stays human-triggered, and no node is made for dismissed work.
Against, and disqualifying on its own: an event queued Monday 23:00 and applied
Tuesday files Monday's work under Tuesday. Silent wrong attribution is the
failure this whole project exists to prevent.

*(c) Apply time, dated from the event's own timestamp.* Everything (b) has, with
(b)'s only real defect removed -- using data the event already carries, since
every queued event is stamped when it is written. Against: Claude never learns
the day-node id during the session.

*Recommended: (c), because its single cost is one this heading has already
accepted.* The paragraph above says so outright -- "Claude never learns the
daily UUID, which is the point", the target being the stable category title
=Review and planning=. So (c) is not a compromise between the other two. It
strictly dominates (b), and beats (a) without surrendering anything the design
wanted.

*Two consequences to know before committing.* =org-clock-default-task= degrades
gracefully rather than silently: the =SessionStart= hook still resolves today's
node read-only and never creates, so before the first apply the default task is
simply unset -- [[id:6ae83993-906c-4d8b-b11e-c91be8996962][6ae83993]]'s
territory, already off the critical path. And a late apply becomes *correct*
rather than convenient: meta-work clocked Monday and applied Friday creates
Monday's node on Friday, dated Monday. That reads odd until it is set beside the
archive behaviour measured the same day on
[[id:29439196-bbb8-4b64-b8d5-3bf9d457bf6c][29439196]] -- =org-archive-subtree=
files by =CLOSED=, not by archive time. "File by when it happened, not by when
it was recorded" is then one principle holding in two places, rather than a
special case invented here.

*DECIDED 2026-08-21 by the user: option (c).* The resolver runs at *apply time*
and dates the node from *the event's own timestamp*, never from "today".

This supersedes the "Two callers, and the asymmetry between them is the design"
paragraph above, which says "=org_clock_in= calls it when the target names the
category, and *creates* if absent". It does not create. =org_clock_in= queues an
event as every queued tool does, and creation happens when that event is
applied, against the day the event was stamped. The =SessionStart= half of that
paragraph is unchanged and still correct: it resolves read-only and never
creates.

*What to build, restated under the decision:* find the =:DATE_TREE:= heading;
=org-datetree-find-date-create= for *the event's date*; stamp =:ID:= and
=:CREATED:= if the node was just made; set =org-clock-default-task=; return the
id. The only change from the original steps is the date argument, which is why
this is a smaller build than the open question made it look.

/Recorded by appending, which is itself/
[[id:3063c3e5-0dd0-4354-8985-271b0313c5d1][3063c3e5]]/'s defect: the decision
sits below the superseded paragraph rather than replacing it, so a reader meets
the stale version first./

:END:
*Shipped 2026-08-24* (=2b9be1c=), built to the decision recorded above: option
(c), apply time, dated from the event.

=claude-code-ide-org-resolve-day-node= takes a TIME and an optional CREATE.
=org_clock_in= now accepts the category title =Review and planning= as its
target and *creates nothing* -- queuing stays a line appended to a file.
=--resolve-item-target= turns that target into a real day node at apply, dated
from the item's own timestamp.

*Verified live against the real TODO.org, read-only:* the category is
recognised; today resolves to =nil= because no node exists and none was made;
2026-08-21 resolves to =758643f2=, the node that actually exists; and =git
status= stayed clean throughout, so read-only really is read-only. That last
check matters more than it looks -- the =SessionStart= caller depends on it, and
a resolver that quietly created would manufacture exactly the
one-node-per-calendar-day problem this heading's design exists to prevent.

*The dating is pinned by a test that fails under the rejected design.*
Reintroducing option (b) -- =(current-time)= instead of the event's timestamp --
fails =test-apply-resolves-a-category-target= and nothing else. The fixture asks
for a node dated 2026-08-17 and asserts both that org wrote =2026-08-17 Monday=
and that nothing dated today appeared, so it cannot pass by accident on a day
that happens to match.

*=:CREATED:= is stamped from the event as well*, not from now. A node minted on
Friday for Monday's work was created, as a record, on Monday; stamping it Friday
would put a date in the drawer that contradicts the heading title directly above
it.

*Nesting is load-bearing and is asserted.* =org-narrow-to-subtree= plus
=find-date-create='s =keep-restriction= keeps the tree inside the category.
Without it org writes a second =* 2026= at level 1, which =bin/lint-org= would
then flag as an uncategorised level-1 heading -- the failure
[[id:95087d8f-e16d-45d3-bdea-9b179dc672b4][95087d8f]] filed after =* *=
swallowed 6462 lines.

*Not built here, and deliberately:* the =SessionStart= refresh of
=org-clock-default-task=. The read-only half of the resolver is what that needs
and it exists; wiring it is
[[id:6ae83993-906c-4d8b-b11e-c91be8996962][6ae83993]], which this heading always
said was off the critical path. Until then the default task is simply unset,
which is the graceful degradation the decision anticipated.

*Now unblocked:* the meta-work spans sitting unassigned in the queue from 08-17,
08-22 and today can be assigned to the category and will file themselves under
the right day. That was the practical reason this went first.


** DONE Institute the Review and planning category, with its DATE_TREE and ritual repeater :code:
CLOSED: [2026-08-21 Fri 13:21]
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       cd1e974e-e006-4974-b770-cdc1cf2e4ab2
:CREATED:  [2026-08-21 Fri 10:10]
:BLOCKER:  e30d52d7-960b-44f3-ab92-93846c81a1bd
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
- State "TODO"       from              [2026-08-21 Fri 10:11] \\
  institute the category once lint is exempt
CLOCK: [2026-08-21 Fri 13:20]--[2026-08-21 Fri 13:21] =>  0:01
- State "DOING"      from "TODO"       [2026-08-21 Fri 13:20] \\
  institute the Review and planning category
- [2026-08-21 Fri 13:20]--[2026-08-21 Fri 13:21] write the category, repeater and archive mirror
- State "DONE"       from "DOING"      [2026-08-21 Fri 13:21] \\
  category instituted, lint and placement verified
:END:
:PLAN:

The act of putting the design into TODO.org. Deliberately last: the user's
instruction 2026-08-21 was that the branch and the real edit belong together, at
the moment the structure lands rather than while it is being scoped.

*The whole initial subtree, as org actually writes it* -- materialized, not
sketched, and comma-escaped per
[[id:8a23d6ec-ca45-454d-b65f-e46ea9204816][8a23d6ec]]:

The shape it created is documented in =.claude/rules/org-conventions.md= under
"The meta-work datetree" -- moved there 2026-08-24 because it is a standing
structural fact rather than this task's history, and because the
=:PROPERTIES:=/=:END:= pairs in the worked example made this body unwrappable:
=:END:= is org's drawer terminator and closes a =:PLAN:= drawer wherever it
appears.

A matching =* Review and planning= must be added to DONE.org in the same edit,
or the =:ARCHIVE:= target is matched as a literal string, fails to resolve, and
org silently appends a second near-identical category at the end of the file.

*The repeater reminds; it does not create.* Chosen by the user 2026-08-21
alongside the on-demand resolver, and the division is load-bearing: creation is
[[id:9575e65b-b589-465a-830c-286541780f96][9575e65b]]'s job and happens when
work happens. If the repeater created the node instead, every calendar day would
produce one whether or not anything occurred, and the id would be re-coupled to
a schedule -- both of the things the resolver exists to avoid. What the repeater
buys is the ritual: a standing daily prompt to actually do review and planning.

*Verified that the repeater and the datetree coexist*, which was not obvious --
the repeater sits at =org-datetree-base-level=, the same level org wants for
year nodes. Materialized with two dates months apart: org skipped the non-year
sibling, inserted =** 2026= after it, and kept month order (August before
December). No clash.

*A category is the one place in this repo a repeater is safe.*
[[id:96a51c2f-ce11-4b40-bdff-11223d3d4eb7][96a51c2f]] records the trap: a
repeater never reaches DONE, so it has no completable parent, and being one made
an epic uncompletable. A category carries no keyword and completes nothing, so
the trap does not apply.

*Blocked on [[id:e30d52d7-960b-44f3-ab92-93846c81a1bd][e30d52d7]] and not on the
others.* That heading's own instruction is to fix the linter "before the tree
exists, not after", because once several hundred warnings are present the
temptation is to silence the check rather than exempt the class. The datetree
tooling can land in either order; the linter cannot.

:END:
*Shipped 2026-08-21*, as one edit across both files: the category with its
=:DATE_TREE:= and =:ARCHIVE:=, the ritual repeater, today's day node, and the
matching bare =* Review and planning= at the end of DONE.org. Placed last among
the categories in both files. Written exactly as materialized above, with one
addition -- today's day node (=2026-08-21 Friday=,
[[id:758643f2-2046-4894-8a25-7ab44e4dad9a][758643f2]]) was hand-created with
=:ID:= and =:CREATED:=, so meta-work can be clocked today rather than waiting on
[[id:9575e65b-b589-465a-830c-286541780f96][9575e65b]].

*The DONE.org mirror carries no =:DATE_TREE:=, deliberately.* Archiving moves a
subtree under the target heading as an ordinary child; nothing runs a datetree
capture against DONE.org, so the property would assert a structure that file
does not have. An archived day node lints there as an ordinary level-2 heading,
since it keeps the =:ID:= and =:CREATED:= it was created with.

*=bin/lint-org= on the real files with the tree present: 0 errors, 23 warnings*
-- the same 23 as before it existed. That is
[[id:e30d52d7-960b-44f3-ab92-93846c81a1bd][e30d52d7]]'s fix verified against the
real structure rather than against fixtures, which is the check that could only
be run at this moment.

*Placement verified against the real file, not a sketch.* A copy was driven
through =org-datetree-find-date-create= for two dates months apart. Both landed
*inside* the category: =2026-08-22 Saturday= nested under the existing =2026-08
August= in date order, and a new =2026-12 December= after it in month order. The
repeater sits at =org-datetree-base-level=, the same level org wants for year
nodes, and org skipped it rather than clashing with it.

*One consequence worth knowing before 9575e65b lands.* Org writes day nodes
bare, so any node org creates that the resolver has not stamped will fail the
lint with =heading has no :ID:=. That is the intended guard, not a gap: a day
node without an =:ID:= exists and is unreachable, and the lint now says so
loudly instead of the node being silently unusable.


* TODO Stale entries in org-clock's persisted history hang the first clock-in after a restart :code:
:PROPERTIES:
:CATEGORY: Clock
:ID:       001fb310-69f7-4417-81be-a224a3bf83e0
:CREATED:  [2026-08-10 Mon 11:51]
:END:

Hit live 2026-08-10 while setting =f9f61c04= to =DOING=. The
=org_set_todo= MCP call timed out, while Emacs itself stayed responsive
to =emacsclient -e= -- a recursive edit still runs a command loop, so
the server keeps answering. That combination makes a blocked Emacs look
like a tool fault.

*Mechanism, fully root-caused:* Doom advises =org-clock-in= with
=+org--clock-load-a=, a =:before= advice that lazily calls
=org-clock-load= on the *first* clock-in of an Emacs session.
=org-clock-load= visits every file named in the persisted history
(=org-clock-persist-file=, here
=~/.config/emacs/.local/etc/org-clock-save.el=). That history still
named =/private/tmp/planning-verify/scratch.org=, a scratch file from an
earlier verification session whose directory /tmp reaping had since
removed. Visiting it produced a buffer whose save asked
=Directory .../planning-verify/ does not exist! Create it? (y or n)= --
and a =y-or-n-p= is *not* an error, so no =condition-case= in this
module could have caught it. It simply waited.

*Why it first looked like leaked state:* a separate =emacsclient= query
reported =claude-code-ide-org--auto-clock-in-active= as =t=, which reads
exactly like a re-entrancy guard that failed to unwind. It was not --
the =let=-binding was still live on the blocked call's stack. Reading it
that way is what located the hang inside =--trigger-auto-clock-in=, and
it generalizes: a dynamic binding observed as set from outside means the
frame that bound it has not returned.

*Cleared 2026-08-10*: =abort-recursive-edit=, killed the stale buffer,
filtered =org-clock-history= of markers into nonexistent files, and
called =org-clock-save= to rewrite the persist file from the cleaned
live history. Verified -- the file no longer mentions the path, and the
next =org_clock_in= returned immediately.

*What is NOT fixed, and is the actual task here:* nothing prevents a
recurrence. Any verification pass that clocks into a scratch org file
under /tmp writes that path into the persisted history, and the first
clock-in after the next restart hangs once /tmp is reaped. =bin/test=
runs =emacs --batch -Q= and cannot pollute the real history, but hand
verification in a live Emacs does exactly this -- and
[[id:3d576d29-747f-40b9-a5b3-ecc76679daad][3d576d29]] established that
live verification is *necessary* here, because batch behaves
differently. Options, unevaluated: prune nonexistent files via an
=org-clock-load= advice; keep live-verification scratch files somewhere
/tmp reaping does not reach; or set =org-clock-persist= to nil and
accept losing clock history across restarts.

Relevant to [[id:b5f7c5c7-7ad6-4c68-9cce-3479db1f1644][b5f7c5c7]]: a
hang in the clock path is the exact failure class that refactor exists
to eliminate, and this one survives the cutover untouched, since the
apply command clocks in through the same advised =org-clock-in=.


* MAYBE Give the review buffer a transient menu, once it has filters worth composing :code:
:PROPERTIES:
:CATEGORY: Apply
:ID:       118373e5-b89b-4001-ad52-1f68374745fb
:CREATED:  [2026-08-07 Fri 15:54]
:BLOCKER:  720b2dcf-6af1-45f3-96a7-aa841e5651e1
:END:

Captured 2026-08-07 from conversation, after pressing =h= in a live
=*org-review*= buffer and finding the help thin. Two cheap fixes landed
immediately — the mode docstring was rewritten as user-facing help
rather than maintainer notes, and =?= now calls
=which-key-show-major-mode= (with a =describe-mode= fallback, since
which-key is absent under =emacs --batch -Q=). This heading is the
*deferred* remainder.

*Availability, checked rather than assumed:* =transient= is loaded, and
=claude-code-ide= itself ships =claude-code-ide-transient.el= with 16
transient prefixes — so it is already the idiom in this exact
ecosystem, not a new dependency. =which-key= is loaded too. =hydra= and
=casual= are not installed.

*Why deferred rather than done:* transient earns its machinery when
there are *options and switches to compose* — that is what makes
magit's popups worth it. The review buffer currently has seven flat
commands and no state to toggle, so a transient menu today would be a
prettier =which-key= at roughly ten times the cost. =which-key= gets
most of the benefit for one line.

*What would change that*, and the real trigger to revisit: the filters
the buffer plausibly wants once it meets real volume — filter by
session, filter to only =(suggested)= items, mark-all-within-a-heading,
apply-with-confirmation. Those are switches, and transient handles
switches far better than a flat keymap does. The
[[id:720b2dcf-6af1-45f3-96a7-aa841e5651e1][720b2dcf]] plan already
named magit-status's staging area as the model, so this is a genuine
direction — it should just follow the filters rather than lead them.
Blocked on =720b2dcf= being used enough to know which filters are
actually wanted; guessing at them now is how the machinery gets built
for a UI nobody needs.

*Promoted to =**= 2026-08-15, evaluated for cancellation, immediate
implementation, or promotion — and promotion is the honest answer.*

=720b2dcf= is =DONE=, so the =:BLOCKER:= is satisfied and this is no
longer *structurally* blocked. It stays =MAYBE= because its own trigger
has not fired: the condition written here is "filters worth composing",
and the review buffer still has flat commands with no state to toggle.
Nothing in real use has demanded a filter. The friction actually
encountered on 2026-08-15 was the assignment prompt
([[id:0d055205-a670-45c6-b4af-980961603a7c][0d055205]]) and candidate
ranking ([[id:49cbe319-f7db-413a-ade8-c4850c1fab8c][49cbe319]]) — neither
of which a transient menu would have helped with.

*Not cancelled,* because the direction is sound and cheap to revisit:
=transient= is already this ecosystem's idiom, =claude-code-ide= ships 16
prefixes of its own, and the trigger condition is written down precisely
enough that a future session can check it rather than re-derive it.

*Not built,* because this heading's own argument forbids it — "guessing
at them now is how the machinery gets built for a UI nobody needs." That
reasoning has not weakened; if anything the two defects found on 08-15
suggest the buffer's next improvements are correctness, not composition.

*Promoted rather than left in place* on the reasoning
[[id:b5f7c5c7-7ad6-4c68-9cce-3479db1f1644][b5f7c5c7]] has now applied
five times: this is a capability that epic *enables* rather than one it
*requires*, and an epic which keeps every downstream capability can never
close. With this and
[[id:0c8644ff-f7b1-4789-ac1e-babf2e9fbc8e][0c8644ff]] out, that epic's
children are 29 =DONE= and 2 =CANCELLED=, and it can close.


* MAYBE Let the review command ask Claude to explain the ambiguous gaps between guideposts :code:
:PROPERTIES:
:CATEGORY: Apply
:ID:       0c8644ff-f7b1-4789-ac1e-babf2e9fbc8e
:BLOCKER:  5ff5a4b8-2fee-472a-b5e1-c9de8cf96704 406e9200-c3e7-49ac-a8d0-d6ff8e061ce4
:CREATED:  [2026-08-07 Fri 08:05]
:END:
:PLAN:

Captured 2026-08-07 from conversation. The idea as posed: while
reviewing a heading in
[[id:720b2dcf-6af1-45f3-96a7-aa841e5651e1][720b2dcf]]'s command, Emacs
offers "Would you like some help with this one?" and, on approval, calls
back to Claude for a recommended aggregation of that heading's
guideposts.

*Transport, researched 2026-08-07 — the protocol answer is "yes, but not
here."* MCP's =sampling/createMessage= is exactly this primitive: a
*server* asks the *client* to run an LLM completion on its behalf, so no
server-side API key is needed, and the spec's own message-flow diagram
puts a human approval step on both the request and the response. It has
existed since protocol version =2024-11-05=, which is what
=claude-code-ide= declares. (Distinct from =elicitation/create=, added
in =2025-06-18=, which asks the *user* for structured form input rather
than the model.)

Three independent blockers, in increasing severity:

- =claude-code-ide.el= implements none of it — zero matches for
  =sampling=/=createMessage=/=elicit= across all ten of its =.el= files,
  and nothing upstream proposes it.
- More fundamentally, it has no *outgoing request* machinery at all. It
  can send fire-and-forget notifications
  (=claude-code-ide-mcp--send-notification=) but has no pending-request
  table and no id→callback correlation, so it cannot issue a JSON-RPC
  request and await a reply. (=--send-ping= is a websocket-level frame,
  not a JSON-RPC request — easy to mistake for evidence otherwise.)
  Adding sampling is therefore "build bidirectional request/response
  correlation," upstream, not "add a handler."
- Sampling only works if the *client* declares the =sampling=
  capability, which Claude Code may well not. Unverified.

*The architectural blocker that outranks all three:* sampling is
server→client over a *live session*. This refactor exists precisely so
the human can review whenever they choose — very often with no Claude
Code session connected at all. The practical transport, if this is ever
built, is shelling out to =claude -p= asynchronously: no protocol work,
no upstream change, works with nothing connected.

*Design objection, and a reframe that answers it.* Recommending an
aggregation is a more confident version of exactly what 720b2dcf
forbids. The danger is not that the model writes anything — it is
*anchoring*: a fluent, plausible-sounding decomposition is far harder to
reject than a transparently mechanical threshold, which degrades the
very safety property the review step exists to provide. Worse, on the
queue file alone the model would have *strictly less* information than
the threshold algorithm — the same timestamps and heading IDs, minus any
domain knowledge — so it cannot outperform the heuristic; it can only
add variance and unearned authority.

The version worth building instead: don't ask Claude to *recommend
intervals*, ask it to *explain the gaps*. Given the session transcript
as input, "what was happening between 10:02 and 10:35?" is a
retrieval-and-summarization task models are genuinely good at and no
heuristic can do at all — and it leaves the interval decision entirely
with the human. That reframing also changes the dependency: the
load-bearing question becomes whether the transcript is in scope as an
input, not whether Emacs can reach a model.

Sequence well after 720b2dcf ships and real queue data exists — there is
no way to judge whether the plain threshold is good enough until it has
been used in anger.

:END:
*That condition is now met, 2026-08-13, and the answer is no — but the remedy is
smaller than this heading assumes.*

The threshold has been used in anger: 23 unattributed spans assigned by
hand in one sitting. The mechanical suggestion built for
[[id:3d0487f4-b8f8-451c-88bf-ab537663cbd0][3d0487f4]] (nearest preceding
=DOING=) managed 18 of 23 and was wrong in instructive ways — it latched
onto a =CANCELLED= heading for eight hours until taught to release, and
offered one heading for a 45-minute span that actually covered five.

*A third source of evidence, discovered by using it before proposing
it.* Asked "how should I identify what a span was circling?", the answer
that worked was neither the transcript nor a model: *git commits and
=:CREATED:= stamps falling inside the span's window.* Three shell
commands resolved three of four spans unambiguously — the 15:30--15:34
span brackets a heading created 15:33 and a commit at 15:34; the
12:36--12:59 span contains three commits and three heading creations.

*Why that beats both options above.* It needs no sampling, so every
blocker is moot. It needs no transcript, so the "is the transcript in
scope" question does not arise. And it answers the design objection
outright: commits and =:CREATED:= stamps are *mechanical evidence*, not
a fluent recommendation, so there is nothing to anchor on. The human
still decides; they simply stop running =git log= by hand. Filed as
[[id:5ff5a4b8-2fee-472a-b5e1-c9de8cf96704][5ff5a4b8]] — a *sibling*,
deliberately, since it shares the goal but none of the mechanism.

*What remains for this heading* is the residue mechanical evidence
cannot resolve: a span straddling two tasks, where commits exist on both
sides and only the narrative says where the boundary fell. The 54-minute
18:12--19:07 span was exactly that shape. So this stays =MAYBE=, its
scope narrowed from "explain the gaps" to "explain the *ambiguous*
gaps". The title was narrowed to match on 2026-08-13; until then the
narrowing lived only in this body, so =org_outline= and the agenda both
still advertised the original scope — which is how a heading gets picked
up later and built to the wrong size by someone reading the index rather
than the prose.

*Re-evaluated 2026-08-15* for cancellation, immediate implementation or
promotion. *Not cancelled:* the residue is real, identified concretely,
and nothing since has made it go away. *Not built:* judging its value
requires the mechanical version to have been used enough to show how
often the residue actually occurs. Six unassigned spans were sitting in
the review buffer that day, but they were unassigned because of
[[id:0d055205-a670-45c6-b4af-980961603a7c][0d055205]] — the assignment
prompt excluding headings created after the span — not because anything
was ambiguous. Evidence about a defect, not about this feature.

*The reframe, which matters more than the promotion.* This heading is
scoped to the *review command* — phase 1, queue → =:LOGBOOK:=. But the
question it answers, "what was happening between 10:02 and 10:35", is
the *attention pass* question — phase 2, =:LOGBOOK:= → attention
intervals, where the human reconstructs a day from evidence
([[id:4f8500e6-b468-4fd4-a0f6-ebf04c7a57ba][4f8500e6]]). That is where a
narrative explanation is wanted and where no heuristic can substitute.
The same conclusion was reached independently under
[[id:f90d745e-ccf9-49d6-a92e-df25c17b4dfb][f90d745e]] — *a recall cue,
not a metric* — which is exactly what this proposes. Re-point it at
phase 2 before building anything.

*One open question got a partial answer on 2026-08-15:* "is the
transcript in scope as an input?" It is readable at
=~/.claude/projects/<slug>/<session>.jsonl= and its structure is
countable — 276 =type:"user"= entries of which 25 were real prompts, in
one session. So the input exists and is parseable. Whether it *should*
be read remains a judgement, not a capability question.

The transport conclusion still stands and is worth not re-researching:
not MCP sampling — there is frequently no live session at review time —
but =claude -p= asynchronously.

*Three details checked 2026-09-01, none of which change the verdict.*

- *0d055205 is DONE,* so the caveat that discounted the six unassigned
  spans has been discharged: the review buffer's unassigned spans are
  now evidence about ambiguity rather than about that defect. The "not
  built" reasoning above asked for exactly this, so the count is worth
  looking at the next time the buffer is open.
- *The =:BLOCKER:= is half-satisfied.* 5ff5a4b8 is DONE; 406e9200 is
  =DOING= and still open, so the heading remains blocked.
- *The "promoted to =**=" note is moot.* Level-1 categories were
  retired, and this sits at level 1 again. The link to =9e627dc0= above
  was also re-pointed: that heading was divided on 2026-09-01 and its
  id travelled with the child, which is now a narrow DONE leaf rather
  than the conclusion being cited. The story is f90d745e.

:PROPERTIES:
:CATEGORY: Clock
:ID:       53b0047d-c55c-486c-8eed-ba4994d97a1a
:CREATED:  [2026-08-04 Tue 18:27]
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
- State "TODO"       from "MAYBE"      [2026-08-14 Fri 13:51] \\
  confirmed surviving; three dependents
:END:
[[file:~/.claude/plans/clock-marker-desync-guard.md][Plan]]

Captured 2026-08-04, hit live during this session's TODO.org commit-
splitting work: writing TODO.org's content directly via =git
hash-object=/=update-index= (bypassing Emacs) and relying on
=global-auto-revert-mode= to pick up the change desynced Emacs's
*in-memory* clock state from the file text. =org-clock-marker= kept
pointing at its old raw character offset, which after the rewrite
landed on an unrelated =:SESSIONS:= line instead of the =CLOCK:= line
— =org_clock_out= then failed outright with "Clock start time is
gone", and the statusline (which reads live =org-clocking-p=/
=org-clock-marker= state, not the file text — see
=claude-code-ide-org--statusline-task-string=) kept showing "clocked
in" even after a text-level recovery via
=claude-code-ide-org-close-open-interval= had already closed the
=CLOCK:= line on disk.

Worked around manually this time: reset
=org-clock-marker=/=org-clock-hd-marker=/=org-clock-start-time=
directly via =emacsclient=, then called
=claude-code-ide-org--clock-status-hook-out= by hand since normal
=org-clock-out= never got far enough to fire it. The existing "Stale
interval recovery" design (CLAUDE.md) only repairs the *text* side of
a stale interval (crash/shutdown case, where Emacs's own in-memory
state is simply gone after a restart) — it has no equivalent for *this*
case, where Emacs is still running but its live clock state has gone
stale relative to a file it didn't itself edit. Whether the real fix
is "never write live org files outside Emacs, full stop" (a
process/discipline fix, not code) or something more automatic (e.g.
detecting the marker/text mismatch and self-healing) is a question for
planning.

*Second, distinct incident, 2026-08-05*: on
[[id:4cda6bf7-441b-47b2-b3d5-b8c7e7a207a5][Show the TODO keyword, not just
clocked in/out, in the statusline]], after a cross-session clock resume (see the
=d150c02e= sibling below) followed by a proper =org_clock_out=, the =:LOGBOOK:=
briefly contained a genuinely malformed line — bare =CLOCK:= with *no* timestamp
at all — sitting between two valid entries, before
=claude-code-ide-org-consolidate-history= rebuilt the drawer and it disappeared
(the arithmetic of what remained checks out, so nothing was silently lost, but
*why* a timestamp-less =CLOCK:= line got written in the first place is
unconfirmed — a race between two near-simultaneous writers to the same drawer is
the working hypothesis, not proven). A second, structurally different way the
same "shared mutable file" pressure can corrupt =:LOGBOOK:=, worth folding into
whatever planning addresses this cluster.

Also worth noting for whoever plans =4cda6bf7='s own fix: swapping
=status-label= for =org-get-todo-state= *at the clock marker* is
still marker-dependent — it inherits this exact desync mode (a stale
TODO keyword shown instead of a stale in/out label), it doesn't
route around it.

*Kept and narrowed 2026-08-13, by the user's decision.* Reviewed against
its two partial owners rather than as keep-or-cancel, because that is
what it actually is: a gap between them.

*What is retired.* The original =org_clock_out= failure is unreachable —
that function opens and closes nothing since
[[id:feba67eb-35b3-48bd-a892-8ecd47ca52e0][feba67eb]]'s cutover. So is
the concurrent-writer =CLOCK:= corruption:
=claude-code-ide-org-consolidate-history= has no production caller left,
only tests.

*What survives, and is the whole of this heading now.* Two surfaces,
each only partly owned elsewhere:
1. =--trigger-auto-clock-in= still
   opens a real clock on *any* transition to =DOING=/=PLANNING=,
   including a human's own =C-c C-t=. Apply suppresses it via the
   re-entrancy guard; a hand-edit does not.
   [[id:ab75d6d2-0e59-405d-92c6-2c67488db133][ab75d6d2]] covers
   *container* headings only, so leaf hand-edits remain.
2. =--statusline-task-string= still reads
   =org-clocking-p=/=org-clock-marker=/=org-clock-history= directly.
   [[id:290b6fc5-3ebb-4827-9a97-5a3542e354b6][290b6fc5]] is scoped to
   the statusline's *data source*, not to marker desync generally.

Cancel this and the uncovered middle belongs to nobody. The deciding
fact against cancelling is that the desync was observed *live on
2026-08-12*, after the cutover, and recorded on =ab75d6d2= — not a
theoretical residue.

*Newly established 2026-08-13, and it raises this heading's stakes.* Reading
=org-clock.el= for [[id:803314aa-eda9-4d48-9e43-b3eb4802a4e8][803314aa]] found
that =org-clock-out= signals =(error "Clock start time is gone")= exactly when
=org-clock-marker='s line is no longer a =CLOCK:= line — i.e. precisely this
heading's desync. That is the same error incident 1 above recorded. So this
desync is not merely a display problem: it is the reachable trigger for apply's
fabricated-interval bug. The two headings are one causal chain, and this one is
upstream.

*Confirmed surviving, and promoted out of =MAYBE=, 2026-08-14 by
[[id:e51d6ba1-cc1d-4745-8aa7-e4b1183a230b][e51d6ba1]]* — the heading
whose job was to decide whether this one is superseded. It is not, and
three separate things now depend on it, which is more than "someday"
can carry:

1. Observed *live on 2026-08-12*, after the cutover, so it is residue of
   nothing.
2. It is the upstream half of the causal chain to =803314aa=, per the
   paragraph directly above.
3. [[id:7771fc63-9374-4c8a-9e80-24ceec21977c][7771fc63]]'s decision to
   *keep* =claude-code-ide-org-close-open-interval= rests explicitly on
   this heading — a shipped decision now cites it as a reason.

*Bit again 2026-09-08, in the milder form this heading predicts, and
the recovery is worth recording.* A session wrote a slice member line
and cookie bump straight to =TODO.org= with a text edit — no MCP tool
adds a member ([[id:635d5abb-207d-445d-b268-bc031bdd592a][635d5abb]]) —
while a human review pass had just written a CLOCK line and its
annotation into the live buffer, unsaved. Result: buffer and disk each
held unique content, so =revert-buffer= would have destroyed the clock
and =C-x C-s= the slice edit.

*No clock-marker desync this time*, because the write touched a slice's
checklist rather than a =:LOGBOOK:= — which is the narrower hazard this
heading names. What the incident shows is the *general* form: any
direct write to a file with a live modified buffer creates a divergence
whose resolution is manual.

*Recovery, for the next time:* dump the buffer
(=write-region= to a scratch path), =diff= it against disk to see which
side holds what, apply the disk-only edits *into* the buffer via
=emacsclient=, verify the buffer is now a strict superset, and let the
human save. Note the last step cannot be automated — =save-buffer= on a
buffer whose file changed on disk prompts "Save anyway?", and a
non-interactive =emacsclient -e= hangs on it, exactly as CLAUDE.md
warns about =@=-cookie keywords. Measured: the call blocked until
timeout while Emacs stayed healthy.

[[file:~/.claude/plans/capture-commit-safety.md][Plan]]

Captured 2026-08-04 from conversation: right now, capturing a new org
heading (per CLAUDE.md's "any time a new task is described in
conversation, create an org heading" rule) only edits the working tree
— nothing commits it, so it sits as an uncommitted change alongside
whatever else is in progress until a human notices and commits by
hand. Tell the org skill to commit the capture itself, immediately,
*given that it's safe to do so*.

"Safe" needs pinning down during planning, but at minimum: only the
=.org= file(s) touched by the capture are staged (never a broad
=git add -A=/=git add .=, per this project's own general git-safety
practice), the repo isn't mid-rebase/mid-merge/otherwise in a weird
state, and — the tricky part — the capture commit doesn't get tangled
up with *other* unrelated uncommitted changes already sitting in the
same file from earlier in the session (a commit should be scoped to
the capture, not sweep up unrelated in-progress edits just because
they're in the same file). Whether that means a targeted
=git commit -- TODO.org= (commits the whole file's current diff,
including any unrelated hunks) is actually "safe" in the sense meant
here, or whether something narrower is needed, is exactly the kind of
question actual planning should resolve rather than deciding here.

Should this be prose guidance in the skill (the model decides "is this
one safe" case by case, same register as the existing read-only-buffer
guidance) or something more mechanical? Lean prose, given the "safe"
judgment call looks inherently contextual rather than a fixed
checklist — same reasoning CLAUDE.md already uses elsewhere for calls
that "only the model can make."

*Update 2026-08-05:* substantial overlap with
[[id:782cda6c-111c-454e-93eb-b2b27012078b][Audit for clock/session bookkeeping
noise before committing during a clocking-suppressed session]] — that heading is
a concrete, evidence-backed version of the exact open question above ("doesn't
get tangled up with other unrelated uncommitted changes"). Not merging them
here; worth deciding during planning whether they should be one task, sequenced,
or just cross-referenced as they are now.


* MAYBE Make org-blocker-hook actually consult :BLOCKER: :code:
:PROPERTIES:
:CATEGORY: Tools
:ID:       087e73e9-bb24-43b6-a972-b7f103d04017
:CREATED:  [2026-08-04 Tue 17:46]
:END:
[[file:~/.claude/plans/blocker-hook-enforcement.md][Plan]]

Captured 2026-08-04, surfaced while adding a =:BLOCKER:= property to
the sibling heading above: this project's own
=claude-code-ide-org--blocker-clock-running-p= (the one function
currently on =org-blocker-hook=) only denies a =DONE= transition while
a clock is running on that same heading — it never reads =:BLOCKER:=
at all, and org-depend itself isn't loaded anywhere in this project
(=packages.el= notes no additional packages). So every existing
=:BLOCKER:= property in =TODO.org=/=DONE.org= (e.g. ID
799b14be-6849-4e22-8a76-4e214d8a25fe's) is purely advisory today —
=org_set_todo= will happily transition a blocked heading to =DONE=
with nothing stopping it, contrary to what the property visually
implies.

*Premise falsified 2026-08-13: org-depend IS loaded and IS on the hook.*
Measured in the live Emacs, =org-blocker-hook= holds
=(org-block-todo-from-children-or-siblings-or-parent
org-depend-block-todo claude-code-ide-org--blocker-clock-running-p)=,
and =org-enforce-todo-dependencies= is =t=. The inference above went
from "=packages.el= adds no packages" to "org-depend isn't loaded",
which is wrong: Doom loads it as part of org, so nothing had to be
added.

Corroborating measurement rather than inference:
[[id:02aaae22-0958-4c0f-b5bb-2cbea32416ae][02aaae22]], =TODO= with three
unfinished blockers, reports =(org-entry-blocked-p)= = *YES*. Something
is evaluating =:BLOCKER:=, and this project's own function does not.

*What is NOT established, deliberately left open rather than assumed.*
A scratch reproduction — two headings in a temp file, one blocking the
other — did *not* reproduce it: neither reported blocked and both
accepted =DONE=. That is far more likely a fault in the test's id
resolution than a contradiction, but it means the end-to-end claim
"=org-todo= to =DONE= is actually denied" is *unproven*. So this heading
is not cancelled: its premise is dead, its goal may or may not already
be met, and settling that needs a reproduction that works.

*A second finding, which the heading would want either way:* blocking is
evaluated against the *blocked heading's own state*.
[[id:0c8644ff-f7b1-4789-ac1e-babf2e9fbc8e][0c8644ff]] is =MAYBE= with an
unfinished blocker and reports blocked=no, while =02aaae22= at =TODO=
reports YES. So a =:BLOCKER:= on a =MAYBE= heading is dormant — it looks
enforcing and is not, until the heading leaves =MAYBE=.

Extend =claude-code-ide-org--blocker-clock-running-p= (or add a second
=org-blocker-hook= function alongside it) to also deny a transition to
=DONE= while any ID listed in the heading's own =:BLOCKER:= property
resolves to a heading that isn't itself in a done state (=DONE= or
=CANCELLED=). Should reuse =org-id-find= the same way the existing
clock-marker check and =org_set_todo='s post-transition heading
lookup already do. Whether to also honor the inverse =:TRIGGER:=
property (documented in the org skill alongside =:BLOCKER:=) is an
open question for planning — =org-depend= itself implements both, but
this project may only need one direction enforced.

[[file:~/.claude/plans/todo-autocapture-dedupe.md][Plan]]

Blocked on the sibling heading above (=Add a PLANNING TODO state,
driven by the ExitPlanMode matcher=, ID
b95b9fba-f78e-48fe-8546-988709cce309), recorded via the project's
=:BLOCKER:= convention (native org-depend syntax; see the org skill
for the full documentation) — not currently enforced by this
project's own =org-blocker-hook= function, which only blocks on a
running clock, not on =:BLOCKER:= (see the sibling heading below for
closing that gap). *Update 2026-08-05: the blocker is now satisfied*
— =b95b9fba= is =DONE=, =PLANNING= is fully implemented, so this
task's prerequisite has landed and implementation could start.
Property left in place regardless (a satisfied =:BLOCKER:= is
harmless under org-depend semantics and preserves why this was
sequenced after that work).

Captured 2026-08-04 from conversation, deliberately at "capture the
idea" depth, not fully designed yet:

Whenever the user says to modify some feature (in the org skill's
scope), first check whether the request matches any existing TODO
heading, including =DONE= ones:
- If it matches an existing heading, ask the user for clarification
  (e.g. "this looks like it overlaps with <heading> — same task, or
  something new?") rather than silently creating a duplicate or
  silently assuming it's the same thing.
- If it doesn't match anything, create a new heading, capture it (via
  =org_capture=), set it to =PLANNING=, ask the user any necessary
  clarifying questions, and then offer to plan it properly (i.e. enter
  Plan Mode). If the user declines planning, revert the heading back
  to =TODO= for later planning rather than leaving it stuck in
  =PLANNING=.

Separately, also check whether the proposed change interacts with any
*open* (non-DONE, non-CANCELLED) task via a depends-on relationship in
either direction — i.e. either the new change depends on unfinished
work, or some existing open task depends on the thing being changed —
and surface that to the user.

Open questions deferred to actual planning, not resolved here: how
"matches an existing TODO" is actually determined (semantic search via
=org_query=? something else?), whether discovering an existing
=:BLOCKER:= depends-on link (this heading's own, above) is sufficient
or whether the check also needs to search prose for informal
cross-references that predate this convention, and exactly when in the
org skill's flow this check should fire.

*Considered for promotion 2026-09-03 while composing =ff7ccb2d=, and held at
=MAYBE= for a reason the satisfied blocker hides.* The prerequisite landing is
not the constraint any more; the *design is stale*. The behaviour specified
above sets a new heading to =PLANNING= and reverts it to =TODO= if the user
declines to plan — and =PLANNING= was **retired 2026-08-28** (=c954f650=), along
with the =ExitPlanMode= promotion hook, after measuring 7 transitions in 432.
Nothing replaced it, deliberately.

So this heading cannot be implemented as written, and the part that survives —
check a modification request against existing headings before creating a
duplicate, and surface depends-on relationships in either direction — needs
restating without the keyword it was built around. That is a rewrite, not a
start, and it should happen before the heading is picked up rather than by
whoever picks it up.

Reconsidered at =ff7ccb2d='s Step 6 (2026-09-08, the user): stays
=MAYBE=, still owed the restating its body names — it is specified in
terms of the retired =PLANNING= keyword and cannot start as written.
Its sibling =3cb3f955= was promoted the same pass.
